# Chapter Summaries

Batch 0001, Chapters 1–10, complete. Batch 0002, Chapters 11–20, complete. These are compact continuity records for the next batch, not substitutes for the chapter text.

## Chapter 0001 — The Bus Token

Adrian runs an ordinary Lot 17 sale on the eastern bank of St. Orra while the clinic's fever shipment sits on that same bank and the Ward Nine ward that needs it is across the water. He refuses to sell a cracked surgical lamp as safe, then a boy of seventeen or near it slips a brass token and torn manifest under the inspection block. Adrian's first contact produces a Tally notice: the Last Fare can open one route for twelve minutes, but no return point is named. It also leaves a metallic taste and a bodily warning. He marks it unsafe, refuses to sell it, and takes it to the back office. In the office he establishes the two threads that run under the batch: the blank lot card Iven Tallow gave him before the Fracture, never once filled and never once readable, and his own memory of the Alder Reach platform where a clean hand in his writing got eleven people off and left four behind. Curiosity and responsibility replace his ordinary control.

## Chapter 0002 — A Twelve-Minute Road

Adrian tests the token against an old Line Six map without activating it, and also sets the blank lot card beside it on the strength of an old Iven instruction; both give him nothing. Repeated contact costs him dizziness, a metallic taste, and a memory gap. The notice clarifies the use, memory toll, driver standing, return point, remembering passenger, and the danger of repeated use. Tarin traces the route, identifies the Ward Nine bell-post platform and its loading spur, and refuses to promise he will remember why he is driving. Adrian chooses not to sell the object or ask an uninformed crowd to vote on it. Mara arrives to audit.

## Chapter 0003 — The Auditor's Question

Mara Quill finds the token absent from the municipal registry and outside the public sale book. She distinguishes custody, sale, and charter, lists the required fields including condition, territory, release, witnesses, and remedy, and refuses to let her inspection count as consent. Halloway Reed argues that delay will kill people. Mara seals the token and manifest at Lot 17, records Adrian's contact, and gives him until first light to produce complete terms. The shipment is on the St. Orra bank and the ward that needs it is in Ward Nine, across the water. Her brother's record contained the phrase “accepted risk,” which she identifies as a label used to end a real question.

## Chapter 0004 — The Safe Bridge

At dawn, Adrian, Tarin, and Mara inspect Selik Marne's official crossing. Selik's bridge is a legitimate, carefully supervised public system, but its named-manifest requirement excludes a feverish child whose guardian cannot be entered cleanly. Mara secures a supervised physical inspection with the bridge terms staying in force during it; **Selik** is the one who identifies the Ward Nine bell-post return point and its one-armed post, and Adrian writes the description on the back of Mara's custody strip. The old shelter by that platform was emptied into the east ward, so it carries rails and a canopy but no children, though people still wait there when a bus is due. The medicine convoy and school shelter group wait at the St. Orra staging yard. Adrian refuses to surrender the token to Selik and cannot yet say whether an honest public opening is possible.

## Chapter 0005 — The Price of the Return

At Lot 17, the crowd demands a crossing. Adrian unfolds Tarin's patched map on the inspection block and draws a fresh small red ring around the east platform, inside the driver's wide one, then explains the outward memory toll and the repeated-use warning but does not disclose the return cost clearly. Mara refuses to close a charter with an unverified return point. Halloway offers a private claim with concealed labor terms. Adrian breaks the municipal seal and activates the token without a valid closing, becoming the bare claimant. Two medicine boxes cross before the return group is diverted toward a false destination. Tarin asks Adrian why he is driving.

## Chapter 0006 — The Memory After the Return

The returning bus is trapped behind a service wall and pulled toward a flooded maintenance bay. Adrian, Mara, Selik's Wardens, and a rescue crew recover Tarin, Dessa Rill, the clinic porter — entered in Mara's affected list by name as **Renn Fisk**, the man who set the six-hour coolant clock in Chapter 5 — and the escort. Adrian suffers exhaustion, numb hand, distorted sound, and memory gaps, and cannot remember drawing the red ring he put on Tarin's patched map. He also briefly receives a fragment of Tarin's own lost memory and notices it is not his; that bleed is unexplained and happened once. Backwash rewrites a clinic slip and a public notice board. He offers Lot 17's repaired blue-collar injector as a concrete settlement, not a replacement for memory. The two boxes that crossed are recovered and brought **back across the official bridge** over the supervised service approach, so nothing has reached the clinic yet. Mara suspends the use, reseals the token, and records the handoff. Adrian promises Tarin to state the full return cost next time. Stage 1 Reader is earned through the failure, promise, and toll echo.

## Chapter 0007 — The Price of a Name

At the Brass Verdict Hall, Mara creates a public complaint and charter-hearing process. Selik demands named manifests; Halloway argues disclosure lets the wealthy buy access. Lina Vale insists the school shelter can be described without publishing a household or the children's names. Mara agrees to a bounded protected-absence channel that keeps the steward, count, bearer duty, and remedy accountable. Adrian admits he wants Mara's procedure because he fears his own improvisation. A message reports a copied Fare claim in use at Halloway's warehouse; Orren arrives with the source trail but not the master.

## Chapter 0008 — The Counterfeit Fare

From the Ward Nine kitchen exchange, Orren traces Halloway's counterfeit. The claim uses a false salt-loading return point and hides a seven-day repair-labor term. He learns Halloway is offering him safe passage in exchange for the master. Orren recovers the **whole** bus manifest — the larger sheet the torn strip at Lot 17 was cut from, lifted during the surge that opened the road — sees a margin note about Sela Pike, and escapes through the Unlisted courier route. He brings the manifest and counterfeit to Adrian and Mara while agreeing to keep Sela's name and the shelter household out of the public book.

## Chapter 0009 — Twelve Minutes of Public Road

Before the public board opens, Halloway's private buyer is stopped with a counterfeit claim. Orren reads the hidden labor term aloud; the carriers refuse movement. Mara states the board's four seats and its limits, then records the claim as invalid without substituting for witnesses — and Mara, not the board, is the one who does the recording, so do not give the panel a refusal or a review it never performed. Adrian writes a one-activation public charter with Tarin as sole bearer, the medicine convoy and nine-child shelter group as beneficiaries, Dessa Rill as the separately named return traveler, a Ward Nine return point named as the old platform **on this side of the water** so it cannot be confused with the far-bank ward, a stated repeat-use risk, release to the public case, and a public appeal remedy. When Alma Dren accepts the witness role and also claims the right to take the nine children over, Mara stops the closing: a witness does not travel on the lot they witness, because the appeal depends on her remembering the terms. Alma stays; two escort stewards take the far-bank duty and give their names on **two** sealed sheets, one held by Mara and one kept by the school, each marked rather than signed, instead of in the book. Ewan Latch accepts as the second witness, and the two acceptances are recorded separately. One of the nine children refuses and asks Alma to state that her name does not speak for him, and a relief driver off the St. Orra staging line gives back a place held open for him. Adrian keeps his hand on the fare box but is not a traveler, so what he pays is the toll echo and the growing distance from his own terms. Sixteen people travel and every one of them pays: eight children, two escorts, and four of the five medicine crew cross and stay, with Tarin and Dessa returning. **Renn Fisk, the porter who set the six-hour coolant clock in Chapter 5 and was pulled out of the wreck in Chapter 6, is identified on the page as one of the four who stay — the same man, crossing a second time.** Tarin and Dessa return; Tarin cannot say why he carried the medicine, Dessa cannot say why she went. The token cracks further, the release is recorded, and the loss walks away from the bus.

## Chapter 0010 — The Public Lot Book

Several days later, Mara formalizes the public lot book at Lot 17 after a review opened at the Brass Verdict Hall. The book records use, toll, bearer, beneficiaries, responsible parties, term, witness roles, release, remedy, and an observed result that accounts for all sixteen travelers, both refusals, and the second payment on the return leg. **Its USE and TERM entries name the return as the old Ward Nine platform on the near apron of this bank, and BENEFICIARIES names the Ward Nine clinic line as on the far bank, so the book itself resolves the two-banks ambiguity rather than leaving it to the reader.** The four-seat public board appoints Nessa Pell steward for seven days in an audible vote. Selik accepts supervised review of his bridge rather than an immediate permanent seizure. Halloway offers to manage future crossings and remains a live pressure. Lina and Orren establish paper copies, and the protected-name rule gains a sealed-page clause for a responsible party who must be identified. Alma Dren says what she can and cannot testify to, which is why the two who did not cross are the two who can carry an appeal. The broken-circle mark on the token, counterfeit, and recovered manifest matches an unread regional record beyond current maps. Adrian signs a promise to disclose the full cost before another activation; Lot 17's inspection press and repair crew remain available, not posted as security. Dessa returns to the depot knowing the route but not her purpose, and the next medicine shipment is due at dawn.

Batch 0002, Chapters 11-20, complete. These are compact continuity records for the next batch, not substitutes for the chapter text. A full beat-by-beat account is in `state/batch-0002-summary.md`.

## Chapter 0011 - The Next Morning's Price

Three honest claimants arrive for one twelve-minute route: the Ward Nine clinic's fourteen cold boxes, the ward market's request for a published three-day service, and Alma Dren's fourteen people stranded on the far bank. Adrian says "the clinic goes first" in front of nine people, hears himself, and stops. He states aloud that he wants to be the one who decides who gets the road, and that the wanting is why there is a board. He refuses the ranking and gives it to the board, which is seated aloud (Mara, Nessa Pell, Coll Ferrin, Marda Osk), refuses to record a preference, and records three requests with no priority. The token's condition is read in public under supervision: **cracked, a second route may not be available**, and the echo leaves Adrian's right hand slack. A boy who refused to board in Chapter 9 offers to be the one who testifies to what the crossings cost. A courier brings two bad pieces of news: Selik's bridge review has been moved up, and a private buyer has paid Halloway for the next crossing and means to take it before dawn.

## Chapter 0012 - The Manifest Question

The supervised bridge review at the Brass Verdict Hall, at a table covered in duplicate bridge forms. Members named aloud: Mara (registrar, not independent), Ansel Roak (route keeper), Ewan Latch and Oveta Kraus (ward market). Selik offers a narrower condition; Mara proposes a supervised group designation with a sealable registrar's page, and Selik refuses it. Delia Fenn brings a year-eight permit to show that the manifest saved her brother's body. **A Warden gauge faults, the span reports movement, and Selik evacuates the deck - everyone off, counterweight locked, no exceptions.** Petra Sill, granted a provisional berth minutes earlier, is turned back down the near ramp with her five-year-old daughter Bel, and returns to the end of the queue unasked. The reading proves to be a false alarm: a fuel can dropped at seven bent the sump float, and a chalked maintenance depth mark on the second pier read at lamplight looked like a hairline crack. The span is sound; the evacuation was still correct. Selik tightens the manifest in the same breath. Mara writes the false alarm and the evacuation in one paragraph so the tightening cannot pass as free caution. Selik accepts the review's written question and refuses Mara's remedy; the review continues on the ninth. Alma Dren takes Petra and Bel into the school gym.

## Chapter 0013 - The Paper Network

Orren traces the counterfeit to a **printing plate, not four forgers**: identical miscut type across claims offered by three brokers in three parts of the city - Ossie Prent (lamp-trimmer, arcades), Jory Slade (rope-maker, river stairs), Hask Vell (writes claims at a tea table on the terraces). He also learns that **the broken circle is not a signature but a claim about a system**, pressed onto unrelated documents - a Warden fuel return, a school ration ledger, a pre-Fracture salt permit - to make paper look like the output of a settlement. He cuts the Sela Pike margin note off the evidence along the sheet's existing crease and gives it to Bett Ruhl, so no household can be found on the same page, and keeps the rest. He prints eleven copies of the third page overnight and gets Sabin Tull to see it before he moves. The chapter ends with a signed claim already in circulation and a crossing intended for first light.

## Chapter 0014 - The People Who Refuse Names

Lina's kitchen exchange, where Mara's seven public columns have been copied onto the wall in charcoal plus three added lines (**WHO CAN SAY NO / WHO PAYS IF IT FAILS / WHO ANSWERS WHEN NOBODY CAN BE FOUND**). Overnight the courier network has made the counterfeit unbuyable. Mara comes in through the stair door past the third barrel and refuses to take a roll. The protected category is argued into shape: a group, a count, a steward, a destination and a remedy in the book, and a paper note of who chose to take part held outside it by people the house chooses. Wenna Keel wants her name written for her missing son; Bett Ruhl wants nothing written. Lina admits out loud, in front of eleven people, that she wants Adrian in the room and would rather he were not needed. **Rell Bode lodges the first challenge against a public lot**: a remedy reached by naming her is not a remedy, because the after is the only part that lasts. The board upholds the challenge in part and will not record any closing whose remedy depends on finding a person who refused to be named.

## Chapter 0015 - What the Driver Knows

Tarin's bench beneath the Needle Road House. Standing is a relationship between a person, a route, and a return point - a named pair, not a property of the token - and a bell post on the ground is a landmark rather than a return point, because anybody can move it. The new return is the second rail joint east of the bell post, under the lamp, where the ballast drops and the plate is stamped, **in the bus of Tarin Kest, coming down from the far bank**, plus a far-side condition for the service cut. Adrian pays for the axle in labor: forty shop hours, wheel nights, and an honest re-rating of nine carts and a hundred and forty salvage lots in the Road House yard, entered in both books before the crossing. Dessa Rill cannot be the remembering passenger - what survived her toll is an old rule for a city she is not in (green is the water, red is lamps) - **and Adrian sees the hole in that argument and does not raise it, because whatever made her dangerous to hold a route went out with the reason. Chapter 19 is where that comes back: Tarin names her, reverses himself in the yard, and takes the liability himself.** Bryn asks to ride and is refused on the grounds that wanting it is not allowed. A second route card shows the pre-Fracture Line Six emergency maintenance bay beyond the river wall, on no public map, which is where the road can come up on the wrong side.

## Chapter 0016 - The First Public Service

Mara writes the **seven-item proposal schedule** (route request with use, destination and count; the toll in the travelers' own words; a named bearer and remembering passenger and what that passenger carries; a term with a stated end; a remedy that works for a person the record cannot point at; two fresh witnesses who are not travelling; a public way to refuse that closes only when the case opens) and runs the first proposal through it herself. She refuses Stenn's permanent grant and refuses Adrian's private guarantee. **The board runs its first closing of its own: a return crossing.** Tarin and Wenna Keel go out and two pay; the eight children, two escort stewards and four crew come home. **Sixteen distinct travelers, eighteen tolls.** The object reports `A TRAVELER WHO HAS ALREADY PAID: NOT STATED`. The eight can name the platform and not one can say why they were sent; Renna Ivers, seven, reports that the toll takes the *why* both times and that she looked for a third thing and it is not there. Renn Fisk, on his third crossing, asks that it be written that the toll is not pain - "the thing it does is politeness." **Mara chooses the hearing over the sealed page:** the remedy is called and recorded as *not discharged*, with the protected category named as the reason and the loss heard but not repaired. The crowd's next demand - fourteen boxes, a second and larger shelter group, four repair gangs, one crisis window - is larger than the charter can honestly carry.

## Chapter 0017 - The Larger Shipment

Selik's housing is certified but the slots stay halved; the official crossing can move four small cargo boxes a day and the named work crew, and cannot move the eleven children without a roll. Halloway offers a complete private package: medicine, crews, and shelter, on a **fifteen-day labor term that falls on him**, written on the first page - and states in the yard that the seven-day counterfeit came out of a room he rents to three brokers, that he sold the first one, and that he cannot shut it down. Adrian reads the token three times in two days, gets less each time, and loses an afternoon; he writes the failure in the public book. He drafts a list of **ways the terms can be misused** and takes objections from Tarin, Mara, Orren, Lina, Selik and Fen Ambrose; Orren's is the return - "say what it costs and who pays it separately." Adrian answers three of seven. Then at four in the morning, with the yard cold and the medicine loaded, **he ranks the yard** - medicine, riggers, children, and the Unlisted and cooperative last - and it is a good ranking, and nobody stops him, which is the change. Halloway's private warning closes the chapter: the public board will be sold to the people who can afford certainty, in daylight, by a fair process, with no dishonest act anywhere in it.

## Chapter 0018 - A Sale That Looks Complete

The breach. At three in the morning, in the back office, with the public board turned to face the wall, Adrian drafts the auction sheet. **The RETURN field reads: the return follows the outward term as an administrative extension of the same use, no separate toll is negotiated, anybody who returns pays the memory toll again.** He also lies to one group in plain language: one marked return means one destination, so the riggers lose daylight walking from the receiving ramp, and the sheet says so. Mara comes in at twenty past three with the lot book and reads his own undertaking aloud from page eleven. Adrian answers with the sentence: *the outward crossing is the emergency, the return term can be written within the hour, and nobody is being deceived because nothing has happened yet.* Nobody accepts it. Tarin: he agreed to a return point, not to a return with no terms in it. Selik: a rule that cannot be read aloud by the people it governs has already failed the people it governs, and he will not hold a flag on a confidence. With the room empty, Adrian writes a **private workshop undertaking** - the inspection press at three hundred and eighty, the repair-crew term at a season, three named hands, four hundred and ten plus a season, forfeit to the remedy on breach - stamps it with his auction stamp, keeps it out of the public book, and puts a line on the sheet reading *security offered in the room, entry in the public book to follow the return term*. He knows exactly what that is and tells himself it is temporary.

## Chapter 0019 - The Full Auction

Two hundred inside, a hundred and forty at the glass, and a hundred more standing in the open yard behind them. Adrian chains the doors, ropes the floor, and runs the seven-item schedule. **There is no bidding:** the clinic posts the cold chain and two weeks of clinic hands, the signal crew post nine hands and a season and their tools, the shelter posts its count and a line letting the steward add a child out loud up to the opening of the case, the ward market posts fuel and lamp rights and a gate clerk, the Wardens post the flag and the route keeper's power. The counterfeit is exposed by a carrier opening the fold - Gerta Lund reads the third page aloud and walks her barrow out - and Halloway reads his own longer labor term in public and concedes where the seven-day version came from. Adrian discloses the outward toll and says the return may cost again, framed as the same administrative term, and says the security in the room but not in the book. **Tarin says no** until the board records that the return is unwritten and that he said so before the case opened, and then offers two honest alternatives: go out with the hole recorded, or do not go out at all. The sale closes as a one-activation charter with the return unwritten. **Twenty-nine are offered and twenty-eight travel:** one of the eleven children refuses and the refusal is deducted aloud in the count, and a repair hand who was never in the count walks to the far bank by the old footbridge under a Warden escort. **Tarin names the passenger himself and then reverses himself in the yard, on the record, and twice:** he ruled in Chapter 15, on a bench with the woman it was about standing there to hear it, that Dessa Rill could not hold the destination, and now says in front of two hundred people that it should have been said in front of two hundred people then - a person with no reason left is not the same as a person who knows the way, and if he is wrong the wrong is his and not Mr. Vale's and not the registrar's. **Bevon Rahl** has the count, the passenger, and the toll read back to him before the token leaves the case. The token reports `RETURN CONDITION NOT IN THIS RECORD` and the board's page records the same. The outward side succeeds: fourteen boxes counted across the opening, nine signal hands walking to the cut, and Fen Ambrose dividing the far side on the ramp - four children in to the school house with two of the stewards, six children back on the bus with the third. **The bus turns with nine people on it** - Tarin, Dessa, one steward, six of the ten children - and the route closes at twelve minutes before it comes out, leaving nineteen on the far bank. The board's page reads, in Mara's hand: *the return is unwritten.*

## Chapter 0020 - The Late Return

The same morning, from twenty to eight onward. Adrian's first three moves are to extend, reprice, and reread; the second one sounds, in an empty yard, like a man repricing his own house. He reads the RETURN field four times and understands that this was not a silence but a sentence put in the place a condition goes, pre-consenting twenty-eight people to a toll they were never told what they were agreeing to. The token itself reports **`RETURN: NO CONDITION IN THE RECORD.`** Mara forces a known-and-unknown list in the yard before he can act. Selik asks for the charter, the security, and the return term in that order and states in front of two hundred people that there is no security on this charter; a repair-crew foreman says Adrian told him the press stood behind it, in a room, and asks whether there will be anything behind those season's wages. Adrian says the breach out loud in daylight, and says the sentence again so the yard can hear what kind of sentence it is. Stenn withdraws the ward market's fuel and reports that the refused three-day service is now what sixty people are asking for. A cooperative clerk asks whether the road will be open again this afternoon for three boxes and a draught. **Mara suspends the public road.** The search finds the marked spur empty, the platform empty, nine sets of wet prints going out and one coming back - and the bell post's one arm **turned to point along the spur instead of across it**, four yards past the joint Tarin has never missed. Nobody has been near the post. The party does not get past the river wall. A public complaint goes up on the notice board in a school hand, written by Teo, the boy who refused, before anyone has agreed what to call any of it.

## Rolling handoff, after Chapter 0020

Adrian is still Stage 1 Reader. He has not gained an ability; he has gained a broken public undertaking, a private asset nobody else can inspect, and a sale that worked. The public road is suspended pending a new proposal. The Last Fare is cracked through the plate. Nine people are unaccounted for between the banks: Tarin Kest, Dessa Rill, one of the three escort stewards, and six of the ten shelter children who boarded. Nineteen are on the far bank: four clinic crew, nine signal hands, four children, two stewards. Three boxes of clinic stores and a draught are due at the near abutment by four in the afternoon. The bell post's arm has been turned and nobody has been near it. Selik's bridge is tighter and more credible than it was; Halloway's counterfeit is a printing plate in three hands; the broken circle is a coat, not a signature; and Rell Bode's challenge is still the question underneath all of it - how a remedy works for a person the record cannot point at.

Batch 0003, Chapters 21-30, complete. These are compact continuity records for the next batch, not substitutes for the chapter text. A full beat-by-beat account is in `state/batch-0003-summary.md`.

## Chapter 0021 - The Search Is a Public Thing

The afternoon of the crossing day. Adrian turns a crowd into a search and discovers the search must be a public process: **a route, an end, a person who answers, and a way to stop it** - the last being the Chapter 16 refusal-window rule applied to a room hunting a bus. Mara Quill enters it as *the rule was never about charters, it was about rooms*. **The board cannot grant a search, only Selik can, and Adrian goes and asks for his own terms**, which is the inversion of the batch. Selik's four additions: a Warden and his marshal go past the wall; a walking party does not go onto the ballast past the second rail ever; the river wall is said in Selik's own words, not Adrian's; the party walks the gravel once and the book records what it did to the place. The search returns at twenty to five with nothing, and nothing was destroyed. **The three clinic boxes go on the supervised cargo exception; Adrian tells the clerk he does not know the position of the draught.** The second party brings a single nailed-heel print out of the platform gravel - small, left foot, worn flat outside, three nails gone, not one of nineteen men's feet, made before the first party walked the gravel, and pointing away from the platform and up the spur toward the river wall. **Nell Sarr then finds a clean arc of broken rust inside the bell-post socket and unweathered paint under the arm: a hand has been in there and swung it, inside a day or two of the crossing.** Adrian enters both facts and refuses to let anybody write a cause.

## Chapter 0022 - What the Span Recorded

Selik Marne, the same day into the night. The three boxes cross and **the draught is refused at the scale as a cylinder outside the clamp's certified load and goes by the river stair by hand, four hours, with two of his men off the abutment on a day when the near-side queue is eleven deep.** The far-bank trades are eleven claims against four boxes a day, a queue produced by the manifest he cut on the seventeenth; he refuses a fifth box on the record, enters the refusal, and sends all eleven a copy of the night's log at his own cost. **The twenty-two letters beat is not in this chapter** - it is the review on the ninth in Chapter 29. The three clinic boxes cross at ten past five with a marshal; the draught is refused at the scale as a cylinder outside the clamp's certified load (a hundred and seventy pounds of flat load, cylinder forty-two) and goes by the river stair by hand, four hours, with two of his men off the abutment on a day when the near-side queue is eleven deep. The certified gauge housing - sealed and signed by two people who are not him - **logged a movement at twenty-three minutes to eight on the morning of the nineteenth, on a closed empty span, on the pilot channel: the water office's fourth trace with no instrument attached, there to prove the tape was running.** It has never moved in nine years. He does the tapes at eight that evening, Vasey comes at ten, and **at about eleven that night Selik tells him to leave the pilot channel attached under a written mark rather than break the certificate, and says he will carry supervised cargo and no passengers on it** - a decision that the copy he sends to the public board at midnight makes unrecoverable, and that a second water-office fitter reverses on the twenty-first, on her own initiative and not at his request. He copies the whole reading to Lot 17's public board at midnight **in his own words, in advance of any request, because a reading nobody can account for is about to be shaped by whoever talks about it first.** He declines to put a Warden hand on the bell post. He applies his own March sentence - *a structure that can report a fault it cannot account for is not a structure I carry passengers on* - to his own paperwork before anyone else can, and knows the March evacuation was right and cost a woman her provisional berth.

## Chapter 0023 - The Maintenance Spur

Late day into night, and the hardest physical chapter. Two young Wardens hear an engine idling behind a service gate on the west side of the depot. **The bus is standing at the bottom of the cut with its nose against the service wall, doors open, engine idling after eleven hours and forty minutes with nobody in the driver's seat, route-control socket empty, the fare-box cover hanging on one screw.** The barrow with the broken wheel from the platform is outside the door. **Nine found: Tarin Kest on foot at the top of a service cut four streets away; Dessa Rill at the spur; one escort steward, marked and on a sealed sheet and unnamed in the prose; and six of the ten children who boarded.** The steward came out backwards because she did not trust herself to turn round, put the six around the barrow with the two smallest under the sacking, had no blanket and no fire for eleven hours, and said so out loud *before anybody is kind to me*. The roll was folded inside her coat. **Dessa has Line Six, has the rules, has the skill, and has lost the reason she is the one who drives the medicine, and nobody can say whether the road took that or the cold did.** One of the six children will not wake and is carried to the depot on a handcart at a quarter past ten. **The far-side condition works and is charged for:** the bus came up out of the cut on the wrong side at 7:38, Tarin could not find the pair at all, he stopped the vehicle, got out, and walked back toward the wall for four streets, and nobody followed him onto it, and the bus went on empty-handed and the eight got off it alive. He says both halves out loud and puts it on himself: the clause saved them and it cost eleven hours, four streets, six children in the cold, and a child. **The cause of the broken pair is the marking moving: a hand was in the socket, inside a day or two of the crossing. Who, how, and why are not established.** **The child dies in the small hours of the twentieth, at an hour nobody in the room was keeping and an hour the registrar has refused to invent; her name goes on a sealed sheet in Mara's coat and nowhere else.** At ten past four in the morning the depot notice board has rewritten itself: four lines in Adrian's own unstamped hand naming Lot 17's inspection press at three hundred and eighty, a season of repair-crew labour with three named hands, and the sentence *entered in the public book to follow the return term* - a sentence that existed in exactly two places, one of which was his inside pocket.

## Chapter 0024 - The Bill Comes Due

Mara Quill's chapter, the next morning. The board sits at half past nine with all four seats named aloud - Mara Quill (registrar, not a vote, not independent), **Nessa Pell (sitting steward, two days of term left, asking in public so nobody can say she was given it)**, **Sabin Tull (barrow-hauler, four refusals in nine days)** and **Marda Osk (tea at the tram arch, no claim on the lot)** - and renews the term for seven days in eleven seconds, on the ground that a term that expires in a crisis can be ended by anybody uncomfortable. Nine copies of the book are in the city by nine o'clock, put there by Mara on purpose, including one carried to a tea table by a man with a barrow. The child's death is entered in the yard as a line with no name in it, and the remedy is **called and not discharged**. The four altered documents are found by comparing copies: the notice board; a clinic receiving slip that has acquired **received, with a loss on the return, unsettled, carried forward**; **two pages of the public lot book that disagree about the security, both in Adrian's hand, neither struck**; and **Mara Quill's own sealed-page register, in which exactly one cell has changed: the children's row, which was page forty-three, now reads forty-four, and a forty-third has appeared dated the nineteenth in the lot's own category with no page behind it, so the register asserts a fourth sealed sheet that has never existed** (the original is in a locked drawer and Mara is the only key; what she read was the fair copy a clerk makes in the ordinary way). She says in public, in her own words, that the registrar cannot certify her own instrument and that the change may have come from her page. Adrian asks for the line to be struck; the board cannot strike a line, only record what happened, and Mara says that in a month it would be a habit. **Warden Culler Bray says the two words *hazard* and *story*.** The token is opened to the yard and answers for the fifth time: `RETURN: NO CONDITION IN THE RECORD. / TOLL ON THE RETURN: NOT PAID. / NEARBY ACTIVE RECORDS: CHANGED.` Adrian loses an hour to the reading and Nessa enters the time. **Mara's proposal: enter the private undertaking as offered and not verified, and hand the valuation to the yard.** Four hundred people price the workshop in public and get **six hundred and twenty-nine coppers** - press 380 less a 9-copper leak found by a cooper's apprentice, plus 258 for a season of three hands off the ward market's own rate sheet, computed twice by a clerk who would not be hurried. The last line of the yard is the repair-crew foreman asking which of the two words his brother is supposed to have been owed on.

## Chapter 0025 - A Second Emergency Exception

Two days later. Adrian writes a nine-line return term at five in the morning and decides at ten past six not to enter it. Stenn asks a third time for a published service and says he is asking in front of sixty people. Adrian proposes a second crossing to fetch the nineteen and runs the seven-item schedule properly: route request, toll read aloud in the travelers' words by Nessa Pell, Tarin named as bearer, term, remedy - and the RETURN field, which reads **RETURN: term held by the reader and entered before the token leaves the case**, which is the Chapter 19 promise again in the shape of a term. The board's page from the nineteenth is still on the crate reading *the return is unwritten* in Mara's hand above Adrian's signature. **Sabin Tull reads page eleven, the board's page, and the new sheet, and says: that is the same act, in the same yard, in the same words, four days apart, and you did not get it wrong, you did it again.** Marda Osk seconds. The four seats refuse: there is no proposal, because a term held by the reader is not a term. **Mara stops it publicly and states that the suspension is the board's and not hers**, because a registrar with a thumb on a scale is the thing she has spent eight years preventing. Stenn and **the tenth hand of the signal crew** (who says he is the tenth and not one of the nine, has a page with every number on it, and has got none of them back) both refuse Adrian's argument, and the argument is good and true: the crowd will read a toll as a refusal. Adrian names **Iven Tallow** in the yard as evidence against himself, saying nothing about whether the man is alive. **Mara says the accusation**: a municipal records office on the far side of the river had a man with emergency authority, a seal, and a gift for reading damaged paper; the shape is a decision that is the emergency, a piece of it written down, the rest to follow, because the rest always costs more delay than the delay is worth; and Adrian has described his out loud twice in his own handwriting and did it again this morning while the board was refusing him. She adds that he had not yet said the sentence that man would have said, and that this is what frightens her. Nobody in the yard accepts Adrian's answer, and she says she is not finished, and announces that she will read the charter of the nineteenth out in full at nine the next morning.

## Chapter 0026 - The Man Who Makes It Temporary

The next morning, the session Mara called. She reads the undertaking, the RETURN field, and **the two refusals that named the problem in advance** - Tarin at four o'clock on the afternoon of the eighteenth, and Selik at twenty past four the same afternoon with his hat on the table - and the refusals stand on the page, with Mara entering 13h25m and 15h05m against a 7:25 departure and **refusing to enter a single interval**, because the interval is a number a room picks and the refusals are a record two men made. Adrian reads out the nine lines he kept in a tin, including that a second payment is *not a smaller thing and is not the same thing and is not stated by this charter to be either*, that if the marking is not where the pair says it is the joint is the return and the arm is not, and that a returning person is asked separately before the outward case opens and may refuse without a reason. Sabin reads it twice and cannot find a fold; Marda seconds; the term is entered **as offered for a future use and not attached to any use.** Adrian then gives the market the only thing he has: a list of four things he cannot do - read the route, predict it, post security, prove his own return term would hold - and **a refusal to put a number on his own loss** while six hundred people watch a rate sheet. He says the charter was not breached and that being a reader is why he reached for a promise. **The room turns to Mara and asks Adrian nothing**, and the difference is not hostility. Mara writes the far-bank notice in the third person, sends it down the river stairs, and it is read aloud on the ramp, in a signal hut, and in a school yard by the foreman, with a woman who can read reading the second half herself. The day ends with the yard unable to agree on a **word** - urgent, scheduled, priority - and Adrian on the rail with nothing to offer, which is the first time in seventeen years he has been in a room in that condition.

## Chapter 0027 - Publishing the Failure

Five days. The whole of the nineteenth goes into the public book in full, including the deferral line, the stamped private undertaking, the six objections in the order they were given, and **a two-page list of the nine things the charter did not contain** - Mara's Chapter 17 line, entered at the front, and Adrian refuses to let the yard decide which of the nine were on purpose. The ward market suspends rather than refuses and says so; two long-standing lots leave; the inspection yard closes for two days while he re-prices 140 salvage lots and finds them **four hundred and ninety coppers below** flood-year figures, because thirty-one were priced by a man pretending he was not in a hurry, and that goes in the book too. A trader brings a damaged feed pump, gets four lines and a gap, says *you used to be quicker than that*, and Adrian agrees; a third reading in two days costs him an hour. Halloway comes with no oiled paper, says he is not here to sell, tells Adrian that the man who sold the fold is not him, and then makes his case: a market with one road in it needs a shopkeeper, not a public process. **On the twenty-ninth Adrian pays**: the yard's own six hundred and twenty-nine with the nine coppers and the two hundred and fifty-eight entered beside his, plus salvage re-priced downward, plus the leases - **one thousand one hundred and nineteen** - and the shortfall in the same entry, **uncomputed and not nil, because a nil would be a number he chose.** He states in public that a payment is spent and a security is lost on a breach, that he is a reader and cannot post security, and that **the charter was not breached and that this is the worse sentence**, because a breach can be entered with two names on it and this cannot. A barrow-hauler's association of eleven meets at the tram arch, has the failure read aloud, and refuses Halloway **because they can check the next one** - the first recorded refusal in the book of a good thing, and Mara says she did not know that was available as a reason. The chapter closes at two in the morning on the first of the month with the first document Adrian has written in seventeen years that does not try to look finished.

## Chapter 0028 - The Second Backwash

Orren Pike, at night. Counterfeit claims are at twenty-one and rising in three barrow yards and on the terraces, and a warning cannot stop a person holding a barrow at four in the morning. **The fourth page is a comparison, not a warning**: the claim in hand set beside what the carrier is signing, ninth line opened out, printed on the back of the ward market's own blank claim stock. It is read aloud at a table at the tram arch at four in the morning by Tove Alard, a cooper's apprentice, and Marda Osk for anyone who cannot read. Twenty-two claims in three nights: fifteen matched, six did not, four of those refused at the table and two walked away, and the twenty-second had an error nobody had printed yet, which Tove puts up with Orren's name spelled wrong. **Bett Ruhl sets the limit**: the service has no address, is described as *the place with the tea*, and nobody prints one line that puts her floor in a public place. Orren accepts it and says the same sentence back to her. Then **Joss Amery, a carter with a sick wife in the fever ward, crosses the footbridge in the dark on a counterfeit with no fold in it at all**, because it is nine flat lines and says the crossing is his and needs no marshal. The barrow goes through the rail at the third plank; he gets the man out and does not try to save the load; the return point is real and flooded; he comes home and **loses three whole days on the second and third of the month** - the fixed order is that he brings the claim to the four o'clock table on the second, is told out loud that the return point is under nine feet of water, and goes over about an hour later - and a coal ration board in the wrong quarter has been changed on the second of the month, and a household with a man in that condition cannot examine him. Halloway then comes to Lot 17's notice board, reads the fourth page twice, says that a published road that failed has made the counterfeit more credible because paper is now checkable and what people buy is speed and legibility, says he is not going to pretend his offer is a way of being good, **says in a public yard that Adrian has built the only thing he has been afraid of in six years**, and then sells a claim in the same yard.

## Chapter 0029 - The Bridge Comes Down

Selik Marne, over days, and the review on the ninth. The certificate is on the table face up and he says in his first four minutes that what he is about to say is not to his credit: he sent two words on a slip at two o'clock on the afternoon of the nineteenth, spent three hours reading a piece of paper to find a sentence he already had, sent the reading to a public board at midnight instead, and was reversed on the twenty-first by a water-office fitter who was annoyed **with the slip and not with him**, because the two words had been enough that night and would have been enough before noon. **This is where the twenty-two letters live**: eleven from the queue and eleven from outside it, with four names in common, checked twice and put in the minutes, and five of them from people who said they could manage without the bridge and cannot. He also stands his ground that he has carried a hundred and sixty people on the strength of the rebuilt housing since the twelfth of March, the day he emptied a sound deck for nothing and was right to. **He obtains the closure by having the certificate withdrawn before the review sits, so the review has nothing left to review but the closure**, and he says so out loud. He refuses a fifth cargo box again and enters the refusals. The ward market's appointee Oveta Kraus votes against with her dissent entered in her own words. **Adrian is called, asks for no procedural answer, and gives the concession that makes Chapter 30 work: a public route can be less safe than a controlled one on any given morning, a page is not a floor, and he will not be the man who says the book was enough.** Mara Quill enters in the minutes that she came to stop the closure and could not construct an argument against it; Selik says that in nine years no registrar has examined this and that her failure is the review. The motion is not the closure but a public inspection on stated days, at the ward market's expense, with the days published and read by a clerk the table appoints, plus a standing order that **one office is responsible for the crossing at St. Orra**. Selik shakes hands with Adrian in the yard and says into the minutes that Adrian's published failure is the only reason his own instrument got examined, because a day a closure is won *because a road failed* is a day the district believes roads are the answer. He says the footbridge is not a crossing and will not be made one. Culler Bray ends the chapter by naming a drafting instruction for a permanently staffed crossing with no expiry, in a room Adrian is not in.

## Chapter 0030 - A Witness Who Is Not Named

Lina Vale. Four documents on the kitchen wall in charcoal: Mara's seven columns, the three added lines, the fourth page in four taped pieces, and Selik's order of the ninth with Oveta Kraus's dissent beneath it. The arithmetic: nineteen people, five on the deck, five crossings, two days, **no roll and no possibility of getting one**, because Selik will not put a name in a book about a footbridge. Lina puts the protected category into public service: a **steward named in the public book** (hers, at the price of eleven years of being unrecorded, and she says the reason - on the night of the nineteenth there was nobody in any book who could be sent to look for the nine), a count split the way the people actually are, a destination, a remedy with a rope and a rule that an unclosed group count is published in the morning, and **a split-strip witness chain with a mark instead of a name**, matched out loud in a kitchen. **She says the hard thing first: a protected category has carried a passage twice and a loss once, and the only remedy for the dead child is a number with her not in it, and nobody will ever be able to check whether the number was right.** She prices the cost as a list, because a list can be written down and a feeling cannot: nine people two hours a day for two days, one coil of rope, two lamps, a floor and a stove for two nights, four coppers of flour, thirty-six hours in total, and the fact that if it goes wrong somebody will come and ask who the steward is. **Colm Vaun refuses to sign a strip, is not argued with, and is on the rope four times anyway; Bett Ruhl rebuilds the design so the person inside the group never touches the paper.** The nineteen come home over the eleventh and twelfth - the signal hut's nine in lots of five and four, the second at half past three with forty minutes of light, the marshal deciding on a lamp what no book could decide; the two stewards over first on the second morning with the roll, and the four children after them - and **the middle plank goes into the cut on the fifth and last crossing**, six feet above nine feet of water, saved by a rope practised for an hour the evening before. The far bank is cut off again that afternoon and a plank is in the water to prove the price. Adrian comes to the reading of the full public book in Bett Ruhl's kitchen, reads the object off the open page and gets `NO CROSSING ON THIS ACCOUNT. / NINETEEN ACCOUNTED FOR BY HAND.`, and Mara says the object has declined to certify the passage and that this is the correct answer and took eleven years and a woman with thirty-one keys. Bett Ruhl's household hears itself counted in a public book without being named and nobody looks at her. Lina imposes a limit on Mara: the entry is accepted, it is thinner than the one she would have written on the eighteenth, and it gets read back in the room with two people in it who are not clerks. Two documents on one wall, two answers, and a Hearth Line that is administratively possible and not dependable.

## Rolling handoff, after Chapter 0030

Adrian is still Stage 1 Reader and cannot post security. He has published the whole failure, paid a figure of his own setting that is not enough, and lost the ward market, two lots, the inspection yard, and most of the standing of his reading. The public road is suspended and **no fifth activation is on the record**; the token is cracked through the plate in public custody under a seven-day term renewed on the twenty-first. **Nineteen people were stranded on the far bank and all nineteen have been walked home over the footbridge on the eleventh and twelfth, and the middle plank of that footbridge is now in the service cut, so the far bank has no crossing of any kind again.** One child of the second shelter group died on the twentieth with no name in any public book. Selik's span is shut to all carriage with its certificate suspended by the water office, and a standing order for a permanently staffed crossing is being drafted in a room Adrian is not in. The counterfeit is a published service on the back of ward market stock and Halloway says it is winning on speed. The pilot line drew a mark at twenty-three minutes to eight on the morning of the nineteenth, on a bridge that did not move, and nobody can say why. The protected channel is in public service with Lina's name on it, and the public lot book has been read aloud in full in a kitchen to a room with an unnamed household in it.

---

Batch 0004, Chapters 31-40, complete. These are compact continuity records for the next batch, not substitutes for the chapter text. A full beat-by-beat account is in `state/batch-0004-summary.md`.

## Chapter 0031 - What the Inspection Costs

Ivo Stenn, the ward market's counting room on the terraces, in the days after the fifteenth. He prices what the order of the ninth asks for in four columns and ends with **NOBODY HAS SAID IT HAS AN END.** The levy figures: ninety-one, forty-four, nineteen, and one nobody has set. A trader with a handcart puts a nine-line claim on his table and **Stenn cannot tell whether the ninth line is fair** - the chapter's frame, and the chapter's last line. The eleven trades meet at half past nine: the copper merchants want a month, a chandler with nine claims in the queue asks why a cost is called a cost and not a tithe, and **the tenth hand of the signal crew** says the sentence the room turns on - the ward market is being asked to pay a monthly bill for an unanswered question, and the order does not say the job ever stops being the Warden's. **Mrs Halder**, the rate clerk, finds in a back ledger an eleven-year **paper levy**: four coppers a quarter off every household of two adults and above, entered as *public notice and printed matter*, producing no printed matter. Stenn funds both the inspection and a **public reader at the market counter** out of it at about forty-one coppers a quarter, calls it *the first honest use of an old dishonest one*, and states the batch's institutional hinge: **a man who cannot examine a claim cannot fund a public process either.** He does not ask the room a question, and says he wants the end of the experiment to be a date because the district has spent a month refusing one. **Selik Marne** comes at half past four, listens twice, asks *whose ninth line is it*, and then says in a counting room, for the first time in public shape, **that what he wants is a permanent office** - because he needs a thing a clerk in a room with a door on it cannot end - and that he has wanted one since the twelfth of March, and that the certified instrument drew a line at twenty-three minutes to eight on a bridge that did not move, and that **the two things are three hundred yards apart and he will not put them in the same sentence.** Stenn writes them on two separate lines, says so while he is writing, and they are not joined. At six the room fills, **all four board seats named aloud** - Mara Quill registrar, Nessa Pell sitting steward whose term from the twenty-first lapsed on the twenty-eighth and was not renewed, Sabin Tull, Marda Osk - and Mara reads Stenn's column aloud for eleven minutes. A trader stops her with a question: *why is a thing I have to pay for going to be read to me by a person who cannot be asked whether it is true*, and she answers that there is no good answer. On the way out, **a trader from the lower terrace puts a second nine-line claim on Stenn's table** and asks whether he has been sold a lie or something merely dangerous, and Stenn cannot tell, and does not sleep well.

## Chapter 0032 - A Table Is Not a Church

Tove Alard, three weeks. The comparison table has read a hundred and forty-one claims: ninety matched, fifty-one did not, thirty-two refused, nineteen walked off. **A woman of the upper terraces comes to the table and can only stand there** - she is on her feet from half past two and cannot walk up the tram apron at four in the morning - and her claim is a good one and its ninth line is legal, and she has understood it every time for four years. Tove finds she has nothing to put on a wall that would have helped her. **Bett Ruhl** names the thing: *a church is a place too*, and a reading in a building the reading was not for does nothing. The batch's service is rebuilt as **disclosure that travels to the person** - eleven rounds already walked by people with their own reasons, a **blank strip** carried to a steward's hand, the steward opens it, a reader is in the room, **no courier has ever been in a room where a reading happened**. **Lina's network is refused as a loophole, by Bett, in front of Orren**, because enrolling couriers turns a rumour into a registry; the strip-writers stay outside it. The reader is **Wenna Keel on a nine-day term**, because the alternatives are a girl of sixteen and a woman of sixty-one. The service is explicitly worse than a table and is the only thing that reaches some people at all. **Hask Vell is exposed by a rent receipt**: a stockinger's wife in the terraces, a three-hour stair, a claim with no fold, and on the way out a **receipt for the second room over the shop on Chandler's Row, nine months of arrears at nine coppers, eleven coppers taken, Vell's own hand** - the best-shaped paper Tove has read in three weeks, true in every line. **Tove prints the ninth line's plain translation under every claim** - *what the ninth line actually says, in plain words, and who is abearing it* - and tells Orren it will create a faster counterfeit, and prints it anyway. Orren tells her what setting type is: the moment she can set it she decides what a claim says, and the number of that is small, and it is the only good news in it. On the twenty-second, past midnight, a household is reached; the woman says nobody had told her the ninth line was the one, and asks **what the money is for**; Wenna Keel says the sentence the volume is built on, and the woman says the room ought to be on the terrace.

## Chapter 0033 - One Office

Selik Marne, over days, and the first published inspection day. The flag on the marked spur has been **re-hemmed by nobody he knows** and he writes *I do not know* in his own book and goes to work. On the twenty-fourth, in a ward market yard, about three hundred people, he asks the district for **one office** - one office responsible for the crossing at St. Orra, no expiry, no replacement, at the ward market's expense - and asks for it **before** he explains it, and gives the credit for *one office is responsible* to his own failure in the first four minutes, in the right order. The four paragraphs are in the chapter, and a clerk appointed by the review table chalks a margin note asking for *revocable*. **Mara Quill names it in a yard, not a chamber: a permanent office is a permanent exception, and the man who has spent nine years refusing exceptions is proposing the largest one in the district, and from inside an office it is a service and from outside it is a claim, and both are true at once.** Adrian asks the second half of the accusation - how a Warden got an order, a certificate, a board and a flag inside nine days - and Selik answers that Adrian has not earned the right to the question and that the second half was answered ten minutes earlier by a flag nobody can account for. **Ivo Stenn's eleven traders stop it**, and not on procedure: a party that benefits from a hearing should not be the party that calls it, the notice goes where, and the ward market will not accept an authority it cannot remove. **Oveta Kraus's ninth-of-the-month dissent is revealed to have been the strongest argument on Stenn's side all along**, and she moves for a renewal clause before she goes home. **Tabled, not adopted, not refused**, at a quarter past twelve. Selik says the district has refused the safest answer available to it, that there are four men who could open a road and two of them are dangerous and one of them is him, and that he is not going to pretend the district's reason is stupid. Then, on his own motion with nobody's name on it, **he asks for a public review of his own order of the ninth**, on the day the ward market votes on the paper levy, and says it is the first time in nine years he has asked anybody to look at his own order. The appointed clerk, a man named Ravell with a cold, reads the days at the end.

## Chapter 0034 - The People the Road Touches

Mara Quill, the school gym on the terraces, the twenty-sixth, ninety-one people and about forty of them unable to read the second half of anything. She has run a process once in eight years and says so. **Rule one: nothing is written in this room that is not read back aloud to the people who will pay it.** The clinic turns out not to be one thing: **Renn Fisk** says a store is not a clinic and puts ninety-four lines of a boxes list on the table, and **Dessa Rill** says she can drive and has no reason for it and asks to be entered as *the hands of the Line Six crew, of whom this one is one* rather than as a sentence. **Fen Ambrose** puts the school roll on the crate: **seventeen, and the name of the group is nineteen**, and the two who are missing went to a sister on the far bank on the second with their mother's signature and a strip under a flour barrel, and **the mother is asked whether she wants to be and says no**, which is the only line in the book recording a refusal of the act of being recorded. A trader proposes putting the far bank in the count under Lina's protected category, and **Lina refuses in front of everybody including Adrian**: the category's only membership rule is that people in it have refused a name, an empty category anybody can use is a levy, and the count is not a remedy. She says the price she paid for eleven years of unrecorded life was that on the night of the nineteenth nobody in any book could be sent. **She then tells her brother, in front of the room, that he has been the right man in the wrong room for a month and she is not going to give him something to do about it.** The session produces **no new term at all** and instead produces a list of the eight the district already has: Tarin's named-pair and far-side condition; Adrian's nine lines; Selik's four manifest terms; Mara's seven-item schedule; Bett Ruhl's strip chain; the no-name remedy; the payment rule with its uncomputed shortfall; and the three lines on the kitchen wall. **Adrian reads all of it aloud in twenty-two minutes** in the flat voice he uses for tolls, and that is the batch's method and his growth. Then Teo asks **the question no charter in the book has ever asked**: if the route never opens, who tells four thousand households, by whom, and for how long - because a man on a terrace is the only man who can tell them the road is coming and he is not lying. Adrian says *I do not know*. It is entered as an open question with no term attached, and **the board publishes it and calls a review**, because a question with no answer is not a result and publishing it is one of the four things a board can do.

## Chapter 0035 - Splitting the Road

Adrian Vale, the back office and the yard, the twenty-seventh to the twenty-ninth. He cannot write a use with more than one person carrying it, and the reason is that **a named pair has a person in it and a charter cannot be a sentence about four men's feet.** He shows the room the problem and solves it in the wrong way on purpose: the person half of the pair becomes a slot - *in a bus of the Hearth Line, named on the day, the name entered before the case opens*. **Mara names it as half a pair**, a synonym for the nineteenth; **Orren names it as the arm again** - a pair with a slot in it is the failure with a piece of paper round it. At the Road House bench, **Tarin Kest refuses to be one of four**: a road that does not stop when the man stops is a queue with a timetable, and the day the rota has a man in it who has never been up out of the cut, he will not know what to do and will not know that he does not know. He offers the alternative - four men who can **drive**, taught in a fortnight, and a pair that is a man who does not rotate out - and asks for his refusal and his agreement to teach to go **in the same entry**. Adrian notices that he has not needed to explain himself for three weeks, and says so. Adrian reads his own draft on the twenty-ninth and gets one bearer's standing and a list: `FOUR BEARERS, ONE LOT. / USE: STATED. TOLL: STATED. / STANDING: STATED FOR ONE OF THEM. / THE OTHER THREE ARE NOT A READING. THEY ARE A LIST.` He states the Stage 1 limit to a yard that does not know what a Stage 1 is. **He enters Tarin Kest's name in the box over his refusal, with the refusal in the same line, and says so in daylight, because the box needs four names and there are three.** The four entries are Tarin, **Renn Fisk** at the near abutment, **a mark** for the far receiving ramp holder, and **Dessa Rill as the remembering passenger and expressly not a bearer** - and a fifth entry that is a rotation of three on foot, which makes Mara say *that is five*. Adrian states in the yard that **he has written a document he is not allowed to close**, that a reader cannot split, bind, close or post security, and that somebody with the power to close it has to read the clauses aloud to the people in it. Published in nine copies before dark.

## Chapter 0036 - The Four Who Have Not Been Asked

Fen Ambrose, the school gym, the far receiving ramp and the yard, the thirtieth to the second. Three hours down the river stairs and three back - the stairs are a walk, not a crossing - with a tally sheet, a pencil with soap in it, and the roll against her body. **Eleven people on the ramp and none of them in a roll, and none of them one of the nineteen, who all came home on the eleventh and twelfth; the shed was cleared on the thirteenth and reoccupied the same week, because the far bank has had no crossing since the plank went.** She finds the fourth entry at the chain, mending a chain with a cold hand, and he says **he does not know what the far-side holder is**, and she enters that as *not a refusal* and says the entry stands until he is told what it is, in words, and decides. At the kettle, **a woman who has not been in a household since she was nineteen** tells her the whole of it: eleven people, four in the school roll, two in the clinic's list, one in the exchange's count, and **the four the count never had** - her, two men who have been on the bank since the fourteenth, and a child of about nine that is hers and is in nothing at all - and that every count in this district is a count of households, so a person not in a household is not missed by anybody, because there is no line to be missed from. She asks that the only thing in the book entered because she said nothing be the fact that she said nothing. Fen comes up the stair and does it in daylight in front of three hundred people: **the count of the twenty-sixth is wrong by four, and the correction is not four names, it is a category that does not exist, and she is not the person to write it and has been certain for nine years that she was.** She uses it against Adrian in front of the room, says she is not calling him a bad man and that the reason she can is that he read her sentence out loud and made it mean something. **The tenth hand finds the same error with his copied page** and calls it the third wrong number in three and a half weeks, and says the other two were in Warden's documents. The four lines are entered: **a person is accounted for by a count and a destination and a remedy and is not accounted for by a household; a group may be a household or may not be; the steward of a group counts the group and may not count the people in it who are not in a household unless they ask; anybody may say no and saying no is not a refusal of service and the count is published with the decline in it.** The wrong count stays in the book beside the right one, because the board cannot strike a line.

## Chapter 0037 - A Remedy for a Person Who Is Not There

Mara Quill, Lot 17, the hearing room and the kitchen exchange, the third and the fourth. **Rell Bode is entitled to the floor and takes it**: a remedy that works by pointing at a person is not a remedy for a person who refused a name, and **a split makes it worse, because the false-destination loss of a split use lands on whoever is standing nearest the fault boundary, and that is a fact about geometry and not about consent** - and the people nearest the boundary are the service-cut yards, four streets west of the depot, in two blocks the ward market has never had a rate sheet for. Mara agrees with all of it in public and offers a remedy she knows will fail: **a hearing on a stated day, a pooled count published before and after with any difference entered as a finding about the counting, and a payment rule that is never entered as nil because nobody came.** She says out loud that she has spent eight years building instruments that work by pointing and does not want this one. The cost is set before the agreement - **two pounds and eleven coppers, entered as nine with the lie in it in the reader's line**, which is the first sum in the book that is wrong on purpose. The remedy is entered **known to fail on the day it is called**, with the words at the front. The board is asked whether it may record a closing on a remedy known in advance to be dischargeable and not otherwise, takes a day, and answers in eleven seconds per seat: **it will record the closing, and it will record beside it the fact that the remedy was entered knowing it would not be discharged**, and that this is the first time the state of the room's knowledge has been entered with the instrument. Nessa Pell adds, on her own account, that this is the first instrument in eleven years that was costed before it was agreed to and the first she has been asked to hold that she cannot check, and that the board may replace her and the remedy will not care. In the kitchen that night, **Mara says the four sentences the entry does not contain** - that the people nearest the fault boundary are the ones nobody counted, that she has known it since the fourteenth, and that the reason entered is going to be true and incomplete - and **Bett Ruhl writes them on the wall under the three lines in charcoal**, and they are the first words in Lot 17's public record entered by anybody who is not a registrar.

## Chapter 0038 - The First Split Auction

Adrian Vale, one day, the sixth. Five hundred people in a yard and nothing to open: no token, no bus, no road. Adrian says it in the first minute and prices salvage for forty minutes to get his hands steady. **The board, all four seats named aloud, constructs a terms-closing out of powers it already has**: it cannot grant a use, so it *publishes a result* whose first line may not be read two ways, takes two fresh witnesses after full disclosure, and calls a review of its own publication. **Nessa Pell built the instrument and gets the entry that says so**, because the man who wanted it did not build it. Seven items in the schedule's order, **with item three ending in the clause Adrian wrote at two in the morning on the second and did not look at again - AND THE MARKED LINE AT THE TOP OF THE SERVICE CUT IS HELD BY THE SAME PARTY FOR THE WHOLE OF THE SAME PERIOD, ON THIS BANK, AND NOBODY GOES PAST IT - which five hundred people argued about for four hours without once arguing about the right thing**, plus **an eighth line, moved by Marda Osk and seconded by Sabin Tull: no goods, no freight, and no carriage of any kind except the person who is carried as a person** - the first clause in the book anybody asked for because a counterfeit's second page made them think of it. The witnesses are **the tenth hand**, who accepts on the record that he is the tenth and not one of the nine, that his nine have been home since the fourteenth, that he keeps a page with every number anybody has said out loud in a yard and has not been able to check one of them, and that if the count is short he will stand at the rail and say so and will not be argued with, and **Bett Ruhl**, who cannot read and says so first and accepts on the condition that the ninth line is read aloud and that she does not have to pretend to have understood it, and asks for a third reading. **Dessa Rill says the batch's first honest sentence about the future**: the paper is the only thing that is real, and she would rather be a line in a book than a driver on a road that does not open, and she has paid five tolls and can price every one and account for none. The record is made at twenty past five, published in nine copies. **At half past six a reader at the ward market counter stops reading aloud, twice** - *Rutt*, a signal clerk of about fifty - first because there are four bearers and she can only find three, and one of the three is entered over his own refusal, and the fourth is a mark, **and then a second time, over the words *the same party*, on the ground that the party it means is a mark and that this one item has that mark holding a ramp on one bank and a line on the other, and that a reader is not going to read out loud a sentence she cannot say who is holding what.** She stops. **The board suspends the closing in its first hour, all four seats named, on the ground that it cannot be performed as written and was not checked.** Nessa's findings number three, and the third is that the board has published a term about a **place** and nobody in that yard has stood on it. Nessa Pell's closing sentence: **a schedule that says the terms are complete is the ninth thing that is not in the charter, and it will be in every one of them, and it is going to keep being in them until somebody with a bad shoulder and a good eye walks out to a service cut and asks the people who live there what they are holding.** Nothing is struck. The paper stays up.

## Chapter 0039 - Four Streets and a Wrong Count

Adrian Vale, the marked spur, the river wall, the top of the service cut and the yards, the seventh and the eighth, in bad weather. **No bus, and what happens is a foot.** The party of six goes out to walk the line because the published terms say the fourth bearer holds the marked line at the top of the service cut, and the line is a six-foot tarred twine between two iron pins above the ballast. **Selik Marne chalks the rule on the rail and stops them: nobody past the second rail, ever** - the fourth term of the nineteenth, with the flag out of the flood year and the twine four years old, all three of them the same rule - and agrees to a look from the twine and no further. What Adrian actually wants is in four documents, and the walk is the least useful part of the morning. **A mark is doing two jobs, in four documents and by three people who never saw each other's document**: the fourth entry of the proposal of the twenty-ninth, the strip Fen made at the far receiving ramp on the second, the published terms of the sixth - which is where the marked-line clause is - and **a paper on a gate in the service-cut yards that says HELD. ASK AT THE YARDS.** Somebody went round on the second, at the same hour Adrian was writing his clause, and got five or six yeses to a household's mark without once asking *may I put your name in a book*. Nessa makes Adrian read out **every place a mark has been used since the fourteenth - twenty-three places, an hour and ten minutes**, and the twenty-third is a strip in a kitchen book that was never meant to be in a public record. **Selik's rule is the rule that saves a man and the rule that puts him in the wrong place:** Adrian's clause reads *and nobody goes past it*, and the *it* is eleven feet above the rail, and a foot of the crown goes down at four minutes past eleven the way a plank goes. **The lamp was still burning, so somebody could see where he had gone.** The Warden of twenty-five admits he was going to tell the man and did not, and Selik tells him he should have, and says to Adrian that he has waited nine years for a man to argue with him on the ballast and it is his own handwriting. **The wrong bearer is of the same protected household as the fourth entry, and the two had never met, and one mark had been given two jobs by two people who had never seen each other's document - the fourth entry of the proposal of the twenty-ninth, and a clause written at two in the morning on the second and published on the sixth.** The man said one thing in Adrian's yard on the sixth, to Orren Pike, in a voice carrying nine feet: *I was sent to hold a line.* **Adrian says first, in the yard, before anybody else, that the schedule is his and the clause is his and it is not bad luck**, and that the man hurt is not in the book by name and the four things he is about to hand over will not help him either. The room uses him for the first time in seventeen years as the man answerable for a document. He is read to the end of his hand. The chapter ends at about ten at night in the kitchen under the tram line, with **Bett Ruhl, Mara Quill and Lina Vale looking at a strip that does not match** - two books, two counts of the same six people, and Lina's rule that **both numbers go in the same book, not the correct one**, and that Mara must stop and ask her in front of Bett Ruhl rather than explain it.

## Chapter 0040 - The Book Opens Again

The token's seven-day custody term, renewed on the fifth of this month, expires on the twelfth and is renewed again in public, on Nessa Pell's own request and on the record - **the fourth renewal since the twenty-first, whose own term lapsed at midnight on the twenty-eighth and stood unrenewed until the yard on the twenty-ninth, which is a day and a bit and not the month Chapter 35 calls it** - and she says out loud that she is going to keep asking for it, and that anybody who thinks four renewals in seven weeks is a sign of anything has not read the entry from the twenty-first. On the eleventh, **Mara reads the whole of the seventh out loud in the kitchen before the table opens, with two people in the room who are not clerks** - the first time in four weeks she has done what she promised - and stops and asks Lina what the two books are, and Lina answers in her own words. On the twelfth the **hearing is called at nine, all four seats named, and is not discharged**: the pool is published before and after at **thirty-five, with no difference**, because a strip chain was matched out loud in a room and the matching found the two numbers that differ, and the two numbers are now both the count, and **the registrar enters, in her own hand, that the remedy's own rule about a difference was carried out for the first time in eleven years and has nothing to do; that the two publications are the same and the hearing therefore has something to work on; that the work was done before the hearing and not in it by a woman with a basket, a cook with a bad hip and a woman with thirty-one keys; and that a remedy which works by counting was disarmed that morning by a kitchen.** **Rell Bode takes the floor and says the thing nobody has an answer to** - a book that records its failures loudly is a book that will be used against the people in it - and Mara concedes it in public and gives the only answer there is, which is that the book has to be fat, because a selective book is the one that cannot be checked, and says herself that this is an obligation and not a solution. **The rewrite lands in five sentences**: use, toll, term, release condition and appeal path in one place in that order on one sheet; the release condition read aloud at the opening and at the closing by a different person each time; any affected person may refuse in public by saying the word and is owed no reason; **no successful crossing may be treated as a transferable ownership right**, and anybody offering a claim on a successful crossing is offering a thing that does not exist;
