The woman of the column wrote under the keeper's figures, in her own hand, that she had stood on that bank for eleven years and been the water for eleven years and had never once been asked what the water cost, and that on the twenty-ninth she was asked, and that the answer she gave was a figure and not a feeling, **and that she would like it entered that she would not have been able to say that figure on the twenty-eighth, and that the difference is a night with forty people on it.** And the entry for the thirtieth: **this district spent nine weeks trying to stop a train costing a village something, and on the twenty-ninth of June a train cost that village exactly one departure out of a hundred and forty-two, which is about a half of one in a hundred, and a half of one in a hundred is the first toll anybody in this basin has ever been able to state, and it is stated because the place it landed on was asked first, and was not asked first by accident.** In the hours the counter was shut on the thirtieth a clerk of the ward market who is not the market wrote the night into the line book in about twenty minutes, and the finding is that it took her twenty minutes to write six lines and that four of the six are the same four words this line book has had since the flood year — the ninth place, the twenty-ninth of June, a place a thing stops at, not a signal, one movement, the origin held, the ninth place — **and under it, in the column for the number of a vehicle, which nobody had ever written in, one word. NINE.** A registrar entered the delay for the whole of the twenty-ninth, the seventh this district has published, on the reason she gives every time, which is that three books in three rooms had to have a night in them and the night happened on a platform nine miles away. And on the morning of the thirtieth, in a yard in Alder Reach, a mechanic of forty-three read the whole of it out of a book to about thirty people of his own: **I signed the paper that made this a place a man could put a post in, and I gave the ground up, and I posted the salvage that is now a platform, and there are nine vehicles standing on a place that did not exist in March, and I have a yard on the tide once a month for a rehearsal and no yard for a cart. I am not asking for any of it back. I am asking that the entry say that a thing a man gives up and a thing a man is given are not the same size and that this district has only ever found out which was which after the giving.**

## Chapter 0099 - A Term of Labour and Stock
The first to the fourth of July, opened on the month turning and nobody in Alder Reach putting a line in a book about it, because the printed timetable prints days of the month and not days of the week and gives the reason on its face, which is that this district does not have a calendar and a day of the week is a thing nobody here can check against anybody else. **The first thing done in the new month was a page going into a book in the hours a counter is shut, and the finding of the first is that a document written on a platform in the rain at about four in the morning can be in a public book in Alder Reach in about nine hours if two people are awake when it comes down the bank.** She wrote the page and the man who had written the answer on the platform read it over her shoulder and did not touch it, and the page carries two hands and the entry says so, and the finding is that **two hands on one page in this district has meant two offices disagreeing for nine weeks and has meant one page for the first time**, and that nobody in the room has said anything about that and it is in the book anyway. The reader at the counter read the whole of it out on the second at about eleven in the morning in the ordinary voice, and about forty people stood there, and a clerk of nineteen years entered that she had read the twenty-ninth of last month's asking out three times and this one out twice and that the difference was that nobody asked her to read the first one and everybody asked her to read this one. And on the second of this month the fourth toll board at the ninth place stopped being the only one with an empty column, and what went into it is three tolls, and none of the three is a figure, **and that is the first time in this district that three amounts have been entered as things that cannot be stated:** a toll that has a name, the standing office of cross-district passage, established, and not at this place, and nobody in this basin can find that office in any register this basin can reach, and it is spelled out on three boards and five hundred sheets; a toll that has a date and no place, the twenty-ninth of June, one movement, the ninth place, and the figure is one departure out of one hundred and forty-two and is not in copper; and a toll that has a place and no name, one hundred and forty out of one hundred and forty-two, the rest being what the ninth place cannot produce. The keeper of that board is a man of about fifty, not paid, not a bearer, and he has said since the eighth of last month that keeping a board is not holding a pair. **He said one thing about the three and it is on the bottom line of the board in his own words, and the sentence is nineteen words long, and the finding of the second is that a man who has kept an empty column for a month has nothing to say about three things in it except that there are now three instead of none.** I have been wiping that column since the twenty-second of last month, and I have been waiting to find out what the argument was for, and there was not one, and now there are three things in it and I still do not know what the argument is, and I would like that written down by a man who thinks he does. He was asked whether he was paid and he said no and the answer went on the board, and it is the second line anybody has written under a heading that says anybody may write one sentence on it that anybody would be asked to explain. The three stations went into the second printing on the second as three places a thing stops at, and the fourth place is on the sheet, and the sheet says beside it that nobody is there, **and that is the first time a printed sheet in this district has printed a thing that is not true next to a thing that is.** A day and a date on the sheet, because a correction that has no date on it is a rumour with a press on it, and **the finding of the second is that a printed sheet which promises one train a month and a correction which promises the same train on a named day are two different promises, and the district owns one of them and has bought the other, and the one it bought came from a man it has argued with in four public rooms.** Three of the four people who got off that train in the dark wrote their names on the sheet afterwards. The fourth did not, and the finding is that a sheet a stranger can take cold is the only instrument this district has ever built that a person can decline to be on, and that this is either the best thing about it or the worst and that nobody in the room could say which. And on the second of this month the rotation turned for the first time under a closed charter, and it turned because the first week ran out, and nobody had to be given a day to make it turn, **and the finding of the second is that a rotation which turns by itself is a rotation and that this district had never had one and had three samples of a subscription and a man who could tell the difference.** The order of the weeks was settled in the middle of last month on the back of a mechanic's pencil on a trade's invoice and has not been touched since; the first week is the keeper's; the second is the man of sixty-eight at the pumping engine, who asked in a room for his week to be called the fourth place and not the Harrow end, and gave a reason that went in the minute, **which is that a week is a place a person stands and not a place a person is, and it costs him two days a month in somebody else minding his engine and he did the arithmetic himself and put the two days in the minute in his own hand**; the third is the signalman of about thirty-one, who refused the week that ends on the twenty-ninth of this month because the third asking was on the twenty-ninth and he was going to be two and a half miles up a bank reading somebody else's question back, and **the finding of the second is that the first reason anybody in this district has ever given for not holding a week is that the week was the wrong shape.** Nobody extended the term and nobody shortened it. It ends on the twenty-ninth of this month and it is written in four places and three of them are nine hours away. And on the third of this month, at a rail in Alder Reach, on a published day, a bridge administrator was asked what his office thought of a charter with four bearers and a toll and a term, and answered in nine words: **I am the office that has been paying a hundred and eighty coppers a quarter on the Harrow works for nine years, and I have not been asked whether that office would carry a week, and the reason I have not been asked is that an office is not a person, and that is my own man's sentence and he wrote it on a board in May and asked for it read out at a counter and not at a rail, and I would like it read out at a counter.** It was read out at a counter on the fourth at about eleven in the morning, in the ordinary voice, in the second clerk's hand and not the ninth hand's. And then the second thing, which he has said every published day for nine weeks and now says with more facts in it and not fewer: **that is not enough. And I am going to keep saying it for as long as I hold this post, and nobody in this district believes I will stop and I am not going to stop.** And the finding of the fourth is that the district has now declined, in public, in a figure, the exact thing that would have paid for the thing it bought instead. **The figure is eighty-eight coppers a quarter, and it is the reason a man at a rail is not a defeated friend and not a friend.** The standing order of the ninth was lost five trades to five trades on the market's own rule, by trade and not by household; eight posts at eleven coppers a quarter is eighty-eight against a take of two thousand and fifty; the market could pay that office more than two thousand times over and has never wanted it, and the reason it has given is that a seat which may be filled and cannot be emptied is a condition and not a service. What the market bought instead was a timetable, five hundred sheets, nine hundred and six coppers quoted on the fourteenth of March, less twelve coppers a quarter of printed matter, leaving a gap of eight hundred and ninety-four coppers it does not have, printed on the sheet in the same type as everything else. **Eighty-eight a quarter against nine hundred and six, and the market could afford both, and it chose the sheet, and a man at a rail has said for nine weeks that a sheet is not a crossing, and the finding of the fourth is that the district did not decline a crossing by accident. It declined it five to five, on a count read out in full, in a room, and then bought a piece of paper with the money it had saved.** Nobody wrote anything under that heading, and a clerk of nineteen years entered that the refusal of the office and the purchase of the sheet are two different votes taken on the same afternoon about two different things, and that the district has been treating them as one argument since March and that they are not one argument. Then the man at the rail did the thing that makes him an ally, in public, and nobody thanked him and he did not ask to be thanked. He asked the ward market, in writing, to pay the man in the hut at the ninth place, and he produced the two numbers in a public book that are about him. **Eleven coppers a quarter for the bench, the lamp, the chalk and the oil. Nothing for the man. And a rate sheet that has carried a post at thirty days since the twenty-fifth of April and has paid it twice without asking.** The second number is not a nil and I want that entered, **because it is not a nil. It is a person at a place that my office has said is unsafe, on a term it renews by itself, for nine days past the date, and nobody in this district has ever costed a signalman and it has costed a pump.** The market refused on the fourth in about four minutes, and the reason is that the office has not asked to be asked and a rate that is not asked for is a rate that has not been agreed to. He asked. He asked you to pay a man and he did not ask the man, **and the difference between those two things is the whole of the last nine weeks and a man at a rail has just been the only person in this district to notice it in nine weeks, and I am not going to be the man who says he was right because he was.** And on the third of this month, in the hours the counter was shut, a printing came off the only press in this district that can do a run in a day, and it is a correction sheet, one sheet, three places, twelve coppers. A correction is owed by a road; that is the man who printed it and it is his own argument and he has been right about it since April in public, in a yard, where he sold nothing. **Three places at four coppers a place. Twelve coppers. The finding of the third is that this district has now got two twelves in one month, and the registrar entered that the twelve coppers of the correction and the twelve coppers a quarter of the printed-matter column are two different twelves, and that the sum of nine hundred and six less twelve is eight hundred and ninety-four and has not moved and is not going to.** He was not thanked and said he would not have been thanked, and he asked for it entered that he was paid, and it was entered, and the entry is the fourth time anybody has asked to be entered as paid in this district's history and three of the four times were his. He came up the bank on the fourth and stood in front of the board with the three tolls in it for about four minutes and said nothing, and then nine words: it costs four coppers to print a place you cannot name. And what does the correction cost. He did not answer it, and nobody answered it for him, and the minute enters that nobody answered it and that the man who sells nothing and has a use said afterwards that he had wanted to answer it and had not, **and that not answering was the correct thing to do, and that the district still does not have an instrument for saying what a toll lands on a place.** And on the fourth a page went into the public lot book with two hands on it that are not his, and the second hand wrote the clause that makes his security inspectable, and the two of them have not said one word to each other about anything since the second of last month: **a security is a thing somebody else can go and look at, and under his name the words *inspectable by any person at any time without asking anybody's permission*, and the registrar is the registrar and not a vote and not independent, and she read it out in the ordinary voice at a counter at about eleven in the morning.** A slate went past in a kitchen under the Ward Nine tram line at a quarter to seven in the morning. Neither of them said a word. A reader at a counter wrote down on the fourth that six people have now seen that kitchen and that she is still not going to ask, because the answer would be in a room and not in a book, and **not asked is in the count.** And the reading ledger was read out at the counter on the fourth: one for April, none for May, and one for the twenty-ninth of last month, and the number of departures he has left is one, and the next permitted hour is not before the fifteenth of this month, and nobody wrote a reason on the page and Stenn wrote one on a separate sheet and read it out at the same counter: **the intervals go up on use and I will not defend it and I will not call it anything else.** The standing instruction that somebody walks every yard of the line is not discharged, and on the third of this month a man and a boy walked it in two days and wrote down every place that reports itself and every place that does not. **The count is twelve places and eleven boards and one place that does not report itself.** The one that does not is the Harrow end: no ground signal, the plinth on the embankment thirty yards east of the engine house with the bolt-holes in it and a cap of lead over them, gone out of the same frame in the fourth year when the iron was salvaged. A signalman offered to put one in a morning on the twenty-ninth of April and was refused in the fastest no of that day, on the ground that a place that reports every morning is a place nobody goes to. **Two men walked the whole line in two days in the first week of this month and the entry says the standing instruction is discharged and not satisfied, because a thing that reports itself and a thing that is walked are two different instruments and this district has now got the second one and still has not got the first.** And the entry for the third also carries the one hundred and eighty-three coppers that went on nothing at the ninth place in the second week of last month, and nobody can say who is owed it, and **the finding is that how a payment reaches a person who is not in a household is still the last open thing in this district that has no instrument, and that a market that has refused an office five to five and printed a gap of eight hundred and ninety-four on its own sheet has not yet found one, and that the entry of the third carries the figure with no answer against it and the answer is not to be struck.** And on the third of this month the Harrow Tongue was entered as a lot with a condition and a keeper, in a remote custody that is not a case and not a counter and not a key, and the keeper is the woman who holds the only pair on that line, and the condition is published in full. A custody with a keeper in it is a custody; a custody whose keeper is entered as nobody is a place where a thing is, and for nine weeks this one has been the second thing. **The boy of nineteen asked for his name on the sheet before the iron's and his name is on the sheet and it is not a keeper's name, and the entry says both of those, and the registrar was asked to keep them apart and agreed and gave the reason she gives every time.** And the fifth search for the office was run on the fourth by four people in four rooms, and it is the first of the five that was not a search for a name, **and the finding of the fourth is that this district can no longer state what the office was for and has not been able to for four months.** The first four were a registrar through the municipal roll of public offices, a bridge clerk through his own office's standing instructions, a licensed valuer through the guild's register, and a sign-writer of sixty-one through thirty-one years of lettering jobs in which he found the phrase twice in his own hand and no office. The fifth looked for the work: eleven trades were asked to write down, in their own words, what a public office does that none of the eleven trades does, and the answers filled two pages and not one of the eleven trades wrote the same sentence twice, and the registrar entered that eleven trades and one office between them cannot agree on a function, and **that a name which four people have failed to find in five registers is a smaller problem than a function which eleven trades have failed to state, and that both of those are the same problem, and that the district has now written the second one down and has not joined it to the first.** And the last thing on the fourth: the name is on three boards, on five hundred sheets, on a security schedule, and in the water office's own book; it is in five registers this basin can reach and it is not in any of them, and **the finding of the fourth is that this district is getting better at writing a name down and no better at finding one, and that the count of places the name is written in has gone from three to five and the count of places it cannot be found in has gone from four to five, and that those two numbers are the only two numbers anybody in this room will be able to check in four years.**

## Chapter 0100 - The Board of Eleven
The fifth to the eighth of July, opened on sixty-one pieces of paper laid out on the floor of a counting room in Alder Reach at about nine in the morning, and a clerk of the ward market who keeps the line book in the hours the counter is shut was on her knees on the boards with a pair of scissors, and a clerk of nineteen years was standing over her, and about forty people were in the room and none of them knew what the sixty-one pieces of paper were. They are the whole of a column. **It is not in the line book, it has never been in the line book, and it is the only record this railway has of what its signals were held for.** There are sixty-one lines in it, and every signalman on this line has copied it out by hand nine times over nine years and put the copy in a drawer because the line book has no column for it. **THE FINDING OF THE FIFTH OF THIS MONTH IS THAT A SIGNAL REMEMBERS A SHAPE, AND THAT THE SHAPE IS A BROKEN CIRCLE, AND THAT SIXTY-ONE PIECES OF PAPER CUT OUT OF NINE DRAWERS IN NINE HOUSES MAKE IT, AND THAT A SIGNALMAN WHO COPIED A COLUMN OUT NINE TIMES OVER NINE YEARS HAS NEVER ONCE DRAWN IT.** A signalman of the district's own crew, about thirty-one, who holds the second of the three weeks, brought the fourth copy in a tin, and is the only person in that room who has been on this line for nine years. I did not draw it because I did not know it was a shape. **I copied sixty-one lines because there were sixty-one lines. That is all a signalman is. If you had asked me in the fourth year I would have told you it was nothing, and I would have been wrong, and I would have been wrong in the way that costs a place its memory.** He laid them out in order and the order is the order they were copied in, and the order is in the drawer. **The finding of the fifth is that the order is the shape and not the shape being the order**, and that it took sixty-one pieces of paper on a floor and a pair of scissors and about four minutes. And then the registrar was asked to join two things, and she refused, and it is the sixth time, and the two things were on the floor of the room and one impression of a mark. **The two lines are the office's name and the shape, and she was standing on the edge of the paper and not on it, and the minute says so, and a man who was there says he noticed that and has not been able to say why he noticed it. I have held them apart five times and I will hold them apart a sixth, and the reason is the reason, and I would like it entered that I have said the same sentence six times and that nobody in this district has ever asked me why, including a man who has stood in that room four times about this page.** He asked for it entered and it was entered, and the entry says in his own hand, on the margin, that he noticed, and that a man who has asked the same question four times and has never once asked why is a man who did not want the answer, and that the two halves of that sentence are about this page and not about anybody in it. And the finding of the fifth: **a signal remembers a shape and a lever carries a mark, and those are two objects doing two different jobs, and this district put them side by side on a floor because side by side is how a person sees a thing, and the broken circle on a water office notice dated the second of the sixth year and the broken circle made of sixty-one entries in nine drawers are not joined, and the page says they are not joined, and it is the fourth page in this district that says so in those words.** The water office notice is in a drawer that is not locked, dated the second of the sixth year, about the metered column at mile one and three quarters, with an impression of a broken circle above the fold line where a name would go, and there is no name on it; it was found on the fourteenth of last month on a board in a hut at the ninth place, behind a book, by a boy of seventeen who was there to copy a line book. Six impressions now, in two lines, and the water office's own numbered form list is complete to the sixth year and does not contain that column's number. **A second impression is not a second answer, and what the sixth one changed is one thing, and the one thing is that this district now knows where the first one is not, and a page in the public lot book says that in those words with a date on it, and the page is the fourth page this district has written for the purpose of refusing to know something and it is the one page in nine weeks that nobody has argued about.** And on the seventh of this month the woman of the column said the thing she said in March and again in the middle of last month, four words long, in her own hand: nobody lays a stone. And the clerk of the ward market entered that a boundary which is two named people and a stated term and a published entry and a hole in the ground that is not a mark is now due to be asked again on the twenty-ninth of this month, from a counting room, by somebody who is not the man who went up the bank twice, and that the asking will be done in a room and not on a bank, and that the woman who proposed it said she would rather be ended by a room than by a flood, and that the room has not yet agreed to end anything. And on the evening of the fifth, in a counting room, a registrar entered a question in the public lot book as a question, with a date on it, **and it is the first question this district has ever entered as an open question, and the finding of the fifth is that a question in a public book is the only kind of sentence in it that nobody can answer wrong**: what is a public office that a signal can remember and a basin cannot find, and what was it for, entered on the fifth of this month, four people have looked for it in a register in two hours and eleven trades have failed to state what it did, the name is on three boards and five hundred sheets and in five registers and in none of them, the name is not joined to the shape on the floor of this room and the two lines are held apart and the reason is on a separate page, **and nobody in this district is to answer this in a guess, because a guess entered in a public book is a thing a stranger can take cold in four years, and this district has had enough of those.** Nobody argued about it, nobody voted on it, the market did not fund it, and the entry says that the market was not asked, and that a market not asked about a question is a market that has not declined it, and that those are different and that the difference is one of the four things this district learned this year and is going to have to learn four more times before somebody can say it out loud in a room. And then four people in four rooms tried to say what the office was for on the sixth, in about an hour and a half between them, **and the finding of the sixth is that four people who have never met gave four different answers and that all four of them were about carrying something somebody would not carry themselves.** A registrar said it was a place a person could refuse, which is a thing a bridge administration has been for nine years without the word. A bridge clerk said it was a place a name could be charged to, which is a thing a line book has been for nine years without the word. A licensed valuer said it was a place a cost could be put in front of before it was paid, which is a thing a rate sheet has been for nine years without the word. A sign-writer of sixty-one said he did not know, and that he had written the phrase twice in his own hand and had a customer for neither. **Four answers, four rooms, four months, and the entry of the sixth says that the four of them may be four halves of one thing and that nobody is to join them, and that the reason given on this one is that they arrived separately and a person who joins four things that arrived separately is a person who has found four things.** And on the sixth a stranger took a copy of the book cold off a board of eleven in the yard of Lot 17, in about two minutes, and did not ask anybody anything, and walked off with four hundred and eleven pages under his arm. **The room let him and the minute says the room let him, and the entry of the sixth says that this is the fourth time a stranger has taken that book cold since the first of last month and that nobody has ever stopped one of them.** The reason nobody has ever stopped one of them is that a book on a board is the only thing this district has ever built that a hostile stranger can use without permission. The line book went to three places in the first four days of this month and all three of them are nine hours apart or more, **and the third copy is the one on the board of eleven, in the yard of Lot 17, in the open, on eleven boards. The finding of the sixth is that a line book a stranger can read is worth more than a line book four keepers agree to keep, and that this district has spent nine weeks finding that out backwards.** One copy is on a shelf in the hut at the ninth place, beside a stove, eleven coppers. One is in the counting room of the ward market. And the span is still shut: the instrument certificate of the crossing at St. Orra is suspended by two people who are not Selik Marne, the middle plank of the old footbridge is in the service cut, and seventeen people are on the far bank, added to on the fifth of last month to eighteen, with a stamped book and no way home. Nobody opened that on the seventh of this month and **the entry says nobody opened it**, and the finding is that a man at a rail has spent nine weeks being the only person in this district who is right about a shut span, and that a district which has a charter and a platform and three toll boards has not once had to open the span, and that nobody knows whether that is luck or a system, and that in about four years somebody will be able to tell. And on the seventh a reading of the ledger was read out at the counter: one for April, none for May, one for the twenty-ninth of last month, and the number of departures left is one. **The entry of the seventh says what the twenty-ninth of last month made him able to do and what it did not, and it is four lines long, and the four lines are the reason this volume ends where it ends.** A binder may write a bounded charter, split a use among several bearers, and close a transfer without changing the object underneath it; he may make an ordinary lot portable, or renewable, under a stated term; he may post a security, and he did, and it is a day a week and three bound copies of a book. He may not maintain a public lot, coordinate more than one bearer, arrange a dispute hearing, or make the consequences of an object visible to a whole settlement, **and this district has no public lot, four bearers and no hearing, and neither of those two facts is a coincidence.** Six things this district has built and cannot name, and the sixth of them went into a public book on the fifth of this month, and not one of the six has been given a name: a rehearsal, a platform, a rail, a correction, a question and a security. **The finding is that a name is the last thing an instrument gets and not the first, and that all six of them were made out of people and dates and books.** The wall in a counting room still has five questions on it under a heading that says NOT ANSWERED, and a sixth that has no name and is not on the wall, and nobody knows what any of them is owed. The woman of forty-five is not asked for a fifth month and the entry says she was not asked and that not asked is in the count, and that the count now stands at four not-askings and one standing refusal, and that the registrar entered that the count is a column and not a protection and that she is the registrar and not a vote and that a column is the only thing she has ever been able to offer anybody. She was asked twice, in March and in the middle of last month, in a room, in front of about twenty-two people, and said no twice, and she is not in the book and nobody has named her, and her second answer was four sentences long and none of them was the nine-word word, and she asked for the column to be on a wall and not in a book about a railway. It is on a wall. And on the seventh, at about a quarter to seven in the morning, a slate went past in a kitchen under the Ward Nine tram line, and neither of them said a word, **and a reader at a counter wrote down that six people have now seen that kitchen and that she is still not going to ask, because the answer would be in a room and not in a book, and not asked is in the count.** The rain had stopped about two days before. **There were about nineteen people on that platform and a boy of seventeen with a slate and a man of about fifty with a cloth, because the bottom line of a board is wiped when it is wet and it had not been wet since the fifth**, and a man of sixty-one was in Alder Reach and not there, and a trader of fifty-one on the lower terrace had sent nine gallons of lamp oil up the bank on a card with a person's name on it and had asked that it be said that nobody asked her either. The lamp was lit by whoever was standing on the platform, and the day was the day, and the day is written in chalk, and a chalk line is not a mark. And then the last image: **a train stood at a platform that did not exist in March, at a place that is a station now because a village of thirty-one was asked on the nineteenth of last month and twenty-five of them said yes and one said yes with a condition and the book says so in six separate figures, and the train had been standing there for eight days, and the line book has nine hundred and six movements in it and one more, which is the twenty-ninth of last month, and nobody has struck the eight days. The toll board at the platform carries three tolls and a name that is not a place**, and the name is at the top of three boards and five hundred printed sheets and is in five registers and is not in any of them, and a man of sixty-one painted four words on that board at the height of a hand, and the man of about fifty who keeps it has said since the second of this month that he does not know what the argument for the column is and that there are now three things in it instead of none. **Adrian stood on the ballast at the far end of it, at about twenty to five in the afternoon, not holding anything, and the only thing he had posted was a day a week and three bound copies of a book, and the day was written in chalk on the board, and the twenty-ninth of this month was written in chalk on the kerb rail, and both of those were things a stranger could read in the dark by putting a hand out.** **He was not the fourth bearer and he was not a keeper of a board and he was not one of the three who held the weeks.** The man who will end the use is sixty-one years old and keeps a frame at the twelfth place two miles west and has held a pair on this line for thirty-four years, and had been in a hut on the night of the twenty-ninth of last month with two other people in it whose names he was not told in advance, and who had put his hand on a lever and said nine words out loud and had them read out to forty people in daylight. Adrian had never met him and had said in a counting room on the eighteenth of last month that he would have said no if he had been asked, and that saying no is not in anybody's hands any more. **The term ends on the twenty-ninth of this month. Nobody can extend it, nobody extended it, and four people who do not like him can revoke the whole of it in a sentence, and the man who ends the use has not been asked whether he will still be there on that date, and the entry of the seventh says he will be, because he comes twice a month and the twenty-ninth is one of the two, and it says that a man who comes twice a month for nineteen years is not a substitute and is not a keeper and is the only person in this district that the railway cannot be sent to anybody else instead of.**

## Rolling handoff, after Chapter 0100 — Volume 02 complete

**The volume's last five items all happened: a boundary walked to a drainage cut and held by two named people and a hole; a train that reached three stations in the rain under a charter that rotates by the week; a reader who posted a day a week and three bound copies of a book and closed a split, which is the Stage 2 change; a wary ally whose span is still shut and whose office was answered in a public figure; and a close on a signal's memory pattern, an entry whose origin is not a place, and a name nobody in the Morrow Basin can find.** The office is not found and is not to be found here. The two lines are not joined for the sixth time and the shape did not produce the name. The Fault did not disappear; it stopped walking on the twenty-ninth of June, and where it stopped is written down with the date, and nobody knows why and a man with a tape measured it. **The first toll anybody in this basin has ever been able to state is one departure in a hundred and forty-two, and it is stated because the place it landed on was asked first.** The last open thing this district has, and the handoff to Volume 03, is not a mystery: it is that nobody can say what a public office that a signal remembers was for, eleven trades could not, four people gave four answers, and the answers were not joined.

---

# Volume 03 — The Hearth Commons

## Chapter 0101 - Where Forms Come From
The eighth, ninth and tenth of July. Opens on a licensed valuer of twelve years laying a guild certificate on a counting-room table and counting twenty-nine columns with a thumbnail. The man who keeps the fourth toll board at the ninth place, thirty-nine, a signalman of a bridge administration, not a bearer and not paid, came down the bank for a form and not for a railway, and his request in his own hand asks for a form with three columns — a day, a place, an amount — before the twenty-ninth of July or a written refusal. **The finding of the eighth is that this district has a fifth instrument it does not have, and that the fifth instrument is a form, and a form is not made in a counting room.** There are five offices this district deals with; four can be written to, one can be asked. The tenth hand draws the distinction: a rate sheet gets a signature because a person agrees to pay; a column with a place is a thing a person agrees to be charged; a form can be printed and a signature cannot. The counting room counts the rate sheet's columns and gets to nine, and **the rate sheet collects money from four thousand and one households and cannot say where any of them are, and the reason it can collect is that a rate is asked for.** Selik Marne answers at a rail on the ninth, on a published day, after saying in advance that it will take four minutes and will not help, and gives the true reason: **every form in this district was written by somebody who needed the paper for themselves, and the reason the fifth thing has not been made is not that nobody knows how, it is that the man who would make it has never been charged for making it.** He says *that is not enough* afterwards, and adds that he would rather be the sixth person on a bank than a man in a room. Adrian refuses to draft a form: **a form is a blank, a thing made before anybody has agreed to anything, and I have no power over a thing made before anybody has agreed to anything.** The valuer's first entry says there is no thirtieth column anywhere; a woman of about sixty with a stick corrects it in one hour — nobody asked the five offices — and the alteration is signed. A boy of seventeen's index carries the train at a hundred and forty-seven days and five dates on one line with no names against them. Four things the district does not have are counted out loud onto a wall under NOT ANSWERED, and the fifth is not on it.

## Chapter 0102 - A Gap Is Not an Office
The eleventh to the fifteenth of July. The sixth search is run by four people, none of them Adrian and none of them the reader, on two lines: do not look for the name, do not look for what it did, look for what it would have had to write down. Four rooms produce four gaps of the same shape: a roll book column headed PERSON NOT IN A HOUSEHOLD, ruled in the ninth year, never written in; a water office ledger with a meter, a date, a place and no person; a guild certificate with twenty-nine columns and not one of them a place; bridge standing instructions with a span, a date and two signatures and no place. **A shape is not an answer, and four books with the same hole in them is a shape.** The registrar refuses for the seventh time, notices she has given a different reason, and gives it: **the sentence has not changed because the paper has not changed.** She then adds a reason she has not given before — the only thing standing between a stranger and a system is a line that says the four of them arrived separately. A woman of about sixty with a stick asks her why, and the answer is about the person who will read it in four years, and **is not an answer to the office**: *I am protecting the person who reads it. In four years he will not believe me, because I am a person and the page is a page, and he will be right.* Ivo Stenn corrects his own finding inside his own finding: the columns are at least nine years old and he has no document that the ruling was on purpose, and asks for both sentences to be entered. **A gap gets copied with the book that has it in it, so the same gap now exists in about seven places and nobody has to do anything for it to keep going.** On the fifteenth the permitted reading hour opens and about twenty people wait from nine until four, and nobody asks him and he does not offer. **A man who has published the hour of a reading is entitled to wait for it.**

## Chapter 0103 - Nothing to Read For
The sixteenth to the eighteenth of July. About two hundred people fill a yard before anybody says what the meeting is for. A man of about thirty-four who mends fencing asks Adrian, in the five things, whether he will read a piece of cast iron and tell the district what a public office was for. A clerk of nineteen years reads the sixth question out herself, for about half an hour, and the finding is that **a price can be published in a yard a quarter of an hour before a man is asked to pay it, and it happened because of two ordinary pieces of administration.** The batch's one System panel arrives in front of about two hundred people: a reading costs about an hour and a cost; the toll on this iron is the origin; an origin takes memory of departure; he has paid it three times and has one left and one is a number and not a principle; **the iron does not hold places, will not name one, will not answer what a public office was for either, and will give him the sentence he already has if he asks for more; a refusal is a reading, is entered, is not a finding and is not nothing, and he still has to walk out of the room and live in the district he did not read for.** He says no in about eleven seconds. The reason is eleven words: **there was no good of reading it tonight**, and he asks for it in those words so that nobody uses a better reason on a day when there is a reason. It is the sixth refusal; a refusal to read is a reading and is entered, and Ivo Stenn's separate sheet says the intervals go up on use and he will not defend that and will not call it anything else. In the yard afterwards: **you cannot read it. So who do we ask.** The first question anybody asks in about two hundred yards in nine weeks is about a hundred and eighty-three coppers.

## Chapter 0104 - One Hundred and Eighty-Three
The eighteenth to the twenty-second of July. A man of fifty-eight who does not read figures counted two hundred coppers out of a pail on the seventh of last month and was given the figure and not the reason. **Six on lettering, eleven on a bound copy of the line book, one hundred and eighty-three on nothing**, and the four coppers for a bushel of white paint are on a line of their own. The asking runs four days: a payment reaches a person by a name and a row; about nine hundred households are in no row; nineteen people at the ninth place are in no household; thirty-one live on ground held by nobody. A slip of paper addressed by description is made in four minutes and **refused by nine of nineteen people, ten said they would take it, four households said they would take it, and the ninth refusal was not about being found and was about being given a job of keeping something quiet.** A man of about forty-one says a name is a thing a person hides behind and a description is a thing a person is caught with. A man of about thirty-four mends fencing corrected the reading-back twice in five weeks and this is the fifth. Twelve people are found on the twenty-first who were in neither column, eleven asleep at two in the afternoon and one who had not understood that anybody was asking him; a clerk is asked to put him in the count of not-askings and refuses, and **a column is a column and not a bucket.** Adrian is asked to close a transfer of the figure to nobody and cannot, because the instrument asks who it is for. On the twenty-second the ward market puts the page in its own book with a heading and no name against it, and the registrar enters that **a line with no name against it is a column and not a payment, and it is now a column that anybody can go and read, and the district has instead built a place where an absence can be read in public.**

## Chapter 0105 - Two Rooms and a Yard
The twenty-third to the twenty-seventh of July. A notice naming the four things that end on the twenty-ninth goes on eleven walls and three toll boards, with the last sentence on it because the woman of the column asked for the room to be on the board too, in the same size. A stranger takes one cold. **The fourth asking is composed in two rooms and neither of the two women is asked in a room: a person answered where she lives is a person who was not brought to the answer.** The counting room is over a chandler's shop and the asker there is a freight clerk of six years who has spent six years saying no and who has never once written down a question; the yard on the tide is lent by a woman of about forty-four who insists the word *free* is in the entry, and the asker there is a sign-writer of sixty-one who has never been asked to read a document out loud. The asking was composed in thirty-one minutes, nine of them the sixth question. The keeper of the only pair came down the bank for the first time in eleven years and gave her answer in her own order. The reading-back corrected a word about a drainage cut. **A rotation is not free; it is only ever free to the person who is asked, and this one cost two rooms and a yard and a woman four and a half hours down a bank and the ninth place nothing at all.** A man of about fifty-one who keeps the only pair says, in nine words, **that is why it works. It is not his.** One of the three week-holders is not there on the twenty-ninth and his week runs out with nobody in it. Elias Rooke is asked what his standing is for and answers: **the standing is for the day it is needed. There has not been one.**

## Chapter 0106 - The Twenty-Ninth of July
The twenty-eighth and twenty-ninth of July. The iron was in the bracket at nine in the morning and still in it at midnight, nobody threw the points, and the train had stood a hundred and sixty-eight days. **An instrument can end without anybody standing up.** The keeper of the board writes that the column is still empty and the three things in it were there for four months and no fourth thing went in. Selik Marne states at a rail that he is not going up the bank and asks for the why to be in his own hand. The boundary is asked a fifth time from two rooms and both women say yes in the same order. **An instrument which either room can end is an instrument that neither room has to, and the room entered the asking without a vote and a thing entered without a vote is the only kind of thing this district has never had to defend.** The security ended at midnight and the man who posted it did not stand on the platform the next morning: **it ended at midnight and I would have been a habit.** Elias Rooke comes, stands in a frame for about an hour, does not put his hand on the lever, and writes the same four words in his own book. He asks Adrian one question on the day, and Adrian says in about nine seconds that he said no before he met him and asks for that to be on the page where he is wrong and not where he is right; Rooke's answer is nine words: **you were not wrong, you just had not been asked.** Nobody said anything about it being a moment. The fourth place gets nothing, and a correction is owed by a road and there was no road.

## Chapter 0107 - Nothing Is Running
The thirtieth and thirty-first of July and the first, second and third of August. Nobody writes a line in a book about the month turning, for the second time in a year. **The thirtieth, the thirty-first and the first, second and third of August contain no movements, and it is the first week since the eighth of March in which that was true.** A column empty because nobody can state it and a column empty because there is nothing to state are the same four feet of board. The district argues for two hours about whether the iron should come out of the bracket and **there is no one in Alder Reach who is allowed to decide it, and a thing that nobody is allowed to decide is decided by a date, and the date is the twenty-ninth of August, and the twenty-ninth of August is now the next day this district owns.** A man of about forty-five who drives salt comes eleven miles with nine pounds of salt in a sack, stands in a public yard for two hours before anybody speaks to him, and asks nine words: can the water at your place be counted. **A person who stands somewhere every day and writes nothing down for a year is the thing he would pay for.** The line is walked: twelve places, eleven boards, one that does not report itself. A stall is measured for the fourth time. The span is still shut. **A railway held by a date and not by a need stops on the day the date says, and on the day it stopped nobody died, and not knowing whether that is good news or luck is the only fact about the line that is in the book.**

## Chapter 0108 - The Fourth Day
The fourth to the eighth of August. **A column with nothing in it is worth more than a column with a price in it for about four days, and the fourth day is the day the keeper starts, and the fourth day came and he started.** Three lines go into the column: the twenty-ninth of June, the ninth place, one departure out of one hundred and forty-two; the thirtieth of June, the ninth place, an amount the place cannot say; and a day, a place, an amount, ruled and empty. **A toll that lands on a place is not a figure and not a name and not a day: it is a place, a day, and either an amount or the fact that the place cannot say one.** He refuses to give a copy, because a thing with an owner is a mark and a mark may not carry a job. **A board can hold a place because a board is not a document and nobody keeps a register of boards.** The same three lines go onto the board at the fourth place in chalk on the sixth, and a fifth place is promised on the eighth. **A board which says where a toll landed is not a place a toll lands.** The line is walked again and the Harrow end still cannot report itself; a ground signal is refused in about four seconds — **a place that reports every morning is a place nobody goes to** — and the boy of nineteen points out that he has been standing there every day since the ninth of last month and nobody noticed for a month. Adrian will not join a line on a board to a line in a market book, and the registrar refuses for the ninth time.

## Chapter 0109 - A Sentence Somebody Said
The ninth to the thirteenth of August. A man of fifty-eight who carries water and does not read figures waits an hour in a counting room and then says that a public office is a thing that takes the weight for a person who cannot carry it and does not make the weight lighter and does not have to. Three others agree, the valuer calling it a toll board, the ward market clerk calling it a market, and a signalman of about thirty-one calling it a railway and saying it last and quietly. **The tenth hand refuses the joining: three things in this district take a weight and none of them is an office.** The registrar refuses to enter it — the tenth refusal — and adds the half she has not said ten times: **the four answers did not arrive separately; they arrived in four rooms on the same day about two hours apart, and that is not separate, that is the opposite of separate.** The man asks for his own sentence to be struck and is refused, because **a sentence somebody said out loud is not a finding and is not nothing, and if I strike this then the only thing left in four years is four gaps in four books and no sentence at all.** Adrian enters under it that a limit of his is not a history of anybody's. The seventh search finds four documents, one in each room, all written on purpose about a person who could not carry something themselves, and none of them mentions the office. A clerk of nineteen years explains the column for things that are not findings and why it exists. On the thirteenth a cart arrives with two men and about nine tons of salt, and the man who drives it asks a keeper of a board nine words: **who do I write this on, when it is a place.**

## Chapter 0110 - A Term and Not a Quarter
The fourteenth to the sixteenth of August. A man of about thirty-one asks whether the hundred and eighty-three can be struck as unpayable, and the market refuses in eleven words: **there is no column for unpayable and I am not inventing one because a man has had an idea in a lane.** A clerk of nineteen years says the harder half: the entry says the answer is not to be struck and does not say anybody is going to look for it. Adrian is asked, in the five things, whether he will hold the figure, and says yes in four seconds and then says what it is: **I cannot make the payment and I cannot close a transfer to a person I cannot name, and what I can do is say the number out loud at a counter on a day.** He is asked what it costs and the answer is the last departure he has, in front of about thirty people who already know the figure. **A responsibility is not a job; a job can be refused and a responsibility can be taken away in a sentence.** The term is to the thirtieth of September and it is a term and not a quarter, because a quarter comes round again by itself. It did not go to the market, because he is asking for a date and a date is not a rate. A slate goes past in a kitchen on the fifteenth and seven people have now seen that kitchen and nobody is going to ask. The four figures of the twenty-ninth are counted and are a count and not a total. On the sixteenth he reads the figure out and a stranger who took a sheet cold asks what he is worth, and the answer is eleven seconds long, and a clerk underlined one line of it and nobody has asked her which. The seventh searches are listed and none of them is an answer, and the question dated the fifth of July is still a question.

## Chapter 0111 - Nobody Signs for a Ton
The seventeenth to the twentieth of August. Nine tons of salt sits in a lane for nine days and a man who does not read figures counts the sacks because nobody offered to. The cart's own paper says nine tons and a hand against a standard that is a shade out of true says nearer ten; **neither is struck, because a district with two figures for a thing that has arrived has two figures and not a correction**, and a licensed valuer of twelve years says a certificate of a weight nobody has weighed is a certificate of an opinion. The ward market buys a hundred and ten pounds at nine coppers a pound — **nine hundred and ninety coppers, on a line of its own and not the nine hundred and six of a quarter and not the eight hundred and ninety-four that is carried.** Nobody in Alder Reach has ever signed for a ton. **The registrar is told on the eighteenth and enters it as a question dated the eighteenth of August with a heading and nothing under the heading, and says a question that has not been answered is not a refusal, and this district has eleven refusals and no questions and that is the worse of the two**, and she puts three people's rate-and-place-and-office sentence in a line in that order with nothing between them and is not asked why. The man who drives salt says the question is about all three and that a man who carries salt for thirty years does not know the difference. A fifth board goes up at the east end on the twentieth with the same four lines and a fifth ruled and empty, and the man who asked for a board that would take a ton is refused in nine words. **The only place the question can be answered is thirty miles east.**

## Chapter 0112 - A Yard Can Ask
The twenty-first to the twenty-fourth of August. The party is composed in a yard, because a yard can ask a person and a board cannot and a form cannot. Nine are asked in the five things and four are not asked, and one of the four is a man of about thirty-four who wrote a woman into a list of people who had been asked when she had not been; **not asked is entered as not asked.** A woman of about fifty-one who keeps the only pair says no in the order she has used since the twenty-ninth of March. Selik Marne is at a rail on a published day, is not asked, and says in his own hand that he said no to a thing nobody offered me, which is the second time in a fortnight. The man who can write terms is not asked to write the party's terms and gives the reason in nineteen words: **nine people in a cart is nine agreements and not one.** Two of the nine are asked again after the sixth question and **one answer changes and one does not, and a person asked twice is not a person asked once and not a person asked three times.** A paperer of thirty-eight changes hers and asks for a man who is not her to be on the second cart, and Orren Pike is asked in a room and writes the party down and says what a party is. The haulier of about forty says the whole of the risk in four minutes: the water is on the planks or it is not. The reading ledger is one for April, none for May, one for the twenty-ninth of last month, and no date for the next one.

## Chapter 0113 - The Last Day This District Owns
The twenty-fifth to the twenty-eighth of August. The notice about the twenty-ninth of August costs twelve coppers because it has two things on it. **The district has three twelves in two months and a four coppers four times over, and none of the five figures is a column.** A boy of seventeen rules a line under the fifth of his five dates and says the thirtieth is the first sentence about a day anybody has written in nine weeks that he has not had to argue anybody out of. The two-hour argument about the bracket produces a third position nobody had put: **the charter is the only piece of paper in this district that ever said who was allowed to decide anything, and it said it for about a month and it is gone.** A man of about fifty-eight who carries water predicts the finding of the twenty-ninth in advance, asks for it not to be entered as a finding, and it is entered in the column for things that are not findings, which has seven things in it after the twenty-eighth. And nobody goes east, because a man of forty-five who drives salt will not lose a season of salt so that a party of nine can miss the day they came for. The twenty-ninth is composed by four people who are not Adrian and is the second day in nine weeks composed by somebody else and voted on by nobody.

## Chapter 0114 - The Twenty-Ninth of August
The twenty-ninth, thirtieth and thirty-first of August. A mechanic of forty-three brings a ten-foot bar at eleven in the morning and stands with it for two hours and does not put it in, and the reason is nine words: **nobody in this yard can say no to me, so it is not a thing I can do.** He puts it down at ten past four and the minute says he put it down and not that he was stopped. **The finding of the twenty-ninth of August is that a thing nobody is allowed to decide is not decided by a date, and the sheet that said otherwise went up four weeks ago for four coppers and was in every way correct, and nobody in this district knew it was correct until the day it was wrong.** A woman of fifty-one who keeps the only pair says the half nobody had said: **a date is the last day somebody can do something, and those are the same day twice.** Elias Rooke comes two miles, writes the same four words in his own book, and asks whether anybody is going to try it again on the twenty-ninth of September. The boundary is asked from two rooms in about twenty-six minutes of which nine were the sixth question, and the term runs to the twenty-ninth of September, and the registrar is asked to join the two things and does not refuse. A woman of about fifty who is the water at the ninth place is asked before the cart goes and says no and asks for the count of not-askings to be left where it is. **The party of nine became eight, a document about people said nine and eight went, and the man who prints it declines a correction.** After the twenty-ninth this district has two dates and neither is about a piece of iron.

## Chapter 0115 - East of Four Thousand and One
The first to the fourth of September. The month turns and nobody writes a line about it for the third time in a year. Two carts and ten people go over a raised plank road at the ninth mile; the water is off the planks; a man about forty says the plank nearest the far end is limewood and that nothing here has grown limewood for a long time, and **nobody entered it.** The party counts about sixty-one of ninety pans under water and gets the number twice. Halla Dree, thirty-eight, four years in the second channel, will not come out of the frame: **if I come out of it the water goes where it wants.** A loom is described for the first time by a person standing in it and **it is described the way a person describes a job.** A woman of thirty-one says what it costs: **a year. Not a thing that happened. A year.** A man of fifty-six does the arithmetic nobody on either bank has done — ninety pans, sixty-one under water, nine with water and no loam, seven with a frame, six with a person in or beside them, one with a frame and nobody in it — and says **the only pan costing nobody a year is the one nobody is standing on, and the only reason that is, is that there is nobody on it.** The salt man says out loud for the first time that what he has been walking eleven miles twice a year for is a number that is not a copper. The train stood two hundred and two days on the first and two hundred and five on the fourth, written down by a boy of seventeen who was nine hundred miles away.

## Chapter 0116 - How a Job Is Described
The fifth to the seventh of September. **Nothing in this district was read for three days and it happened because four people described a loom and not one of them could be asked a question about it.** A woman of forty-four who has done it eleven years says there was no sixth year. A man of twenty-six who has been in a bed of brine for a year and a half says **I am the loom**, and says he has lost the year before and has been asked about the water about nine hundred times. A man of fifty-two who came out of a channel in the fourth month of this year after four years can tell you the height of the water in any month and cannot tell you what he had for supper on the eleventh of June of the second year, and says **the only thing about me that is a loss and I have never once been asked about it.** A man of fifty-two says **the year is not the seller's.** A fourth description of a person who stands somewhere every day and writes nothing arrives from a person who has never heard of the boy at the fourth place, and **the district joins none of the four.** The reading is asked in the five things and a man of about thirty-four who mends fencing says what it costs, and the batch's one System panel arrives in a field in front of nineteen people: a loom is a thing and a use and a bearer and a toll and not one of the four can be filled in with a person standing in it; a channel may not be split and a year may not be transferred and a year is not an asset and not a security; a person bound to a channel is not a bearer; a person who refused may not be counted as a willing witness; and a certificate four people signed and one of whom did not attend is one certificate and one absence. He refuses in four seconds. **The cost of a reading is a part of the lot's toll, and the toll on this one is a year out of a woman of thirty-one.** The seventh refusal, entered, and Stenn's sheet is read out in a cart.

## Chapter 0117 - Not a Lot You Can Carry
The eighth to the eleventh of September. He writes the most complete set of terms this district has ever seen for a thing that cannot be a lot, and **every one of the five parts in it is correct.** One purchaser, one price of two thousand coppers worked out in eleven seconds off the price of salt, one security that is a thing somebody else can go and look at, one signature. A man of fifty-six asks the only question anybody asks about the document: **who is going to stand in it.** He establishes in public that he cannot be the bearer, because the toll falls on the bearer and the toll is a year of a worker's memory. **A lot with no bearer is not a lot, and a thing that is not a lot cannot be sold, and he wrote the terms four days before he established that and said so in a room.** A woman of forty-four who has done it eleven years reads back the first page and refuses to read the second, **and the finding is that a document can be correct in every line and still be missing the only line that matters.** A guild inspector of forty-four arrives, thirty-four miles, on a letter from a licensed valuer of twelve years who would not travel, and says a signature has always meant the person was in the room and that **this is not a fault in the form, and it is the only thing this district has found in nine weeks that is not a fault in anybody.** A lot has seven parts and not five and the inspector counts them aloud, and he offers to certify a shed if nobody will help him.

## Chapter 0118 - A Price and a Body
The twelfth to the fourteenth of September. He asks a woman of thirty-one in the five things and she answers four sentences over about eleven minutes and asks for the entry to say she answered the last one because it was badly asked. He offers a price and she gives the sentence of the batch: **a price is a number a person can hand over; I am not a number and I am not something a man hands over, and if you buy that frame you will own a thing that needs me in it, and in four months you will be standing in a yard asking me why it has stopped working, and I will have to tell you that I cannot remember last year.** And then nine words: **the price is not the thing, the body is the thing.** **He offers a price four times in four minutes and is refused four times in the same four sentences, and the finding is that an item's price cannot separate the person whose body makes it work, and the price was never the thing and he knew it was not the thing on the eighth of September.** He then says the fifth of his limits: a transfer needs a person to transfer from and a person to transfer to, the frame is held by nine households and not by a person, the bed is a hole, and the year is in neither of them, and **binding cannot create consent and it cannot create a person.** A consent sheet for a two-day test has four signatures, one of them a man's mark he has used since he was nine, and one of the four women signed it two days' walk away at a market and understood it in four minutes and did not take it back. **The terms go into a folder with no name on it.** The salt man says the price is nine coppers a pound and that he was silent on purpose for four days so that a stranger could do the difficult part.

## Chapter 0119 - Four Names and One Room
The fifteenth to the eighteenth of September. The inspector certifies the consent in about nine minutes and **the certificate is valid, and validity and truth are two different things.** A form with twenty-nine columns has four columns for two people and no column for whether a person was in the room, and a signature has always meant the person was there. **Nothing has been forged: four people were asked, four people answered, and one of the four was not in the room, and the form asks whether they consented and they did.** Adrian says the fifth limit he came to say: **he cannot strike it, because a strike is a thing an instrument does and a Binder has five powers and a strike of another body's instrument is not one of them.** The registrar refuses — the twelfth refusal, three parts, and she will put the qualifying line in the column for things that are not findings and not in the book, **because people read the top of a page** — and the finding is that **a book that a stranger can go and read is the only instrument this district owns and it has never once been able to reach a thing outside the room it is read in, and this is the first time that has cost a person a year.** The channel runs for two days and **a year does not go twice.** The inspector certifies a second time on the same form recording the defect, and it is also valid. **The only way to be wrong about a consent in this basin is to be right twice.** A man of fifty-two says **the year is not the seller's** and asks that it be in a hand a woman of thirty-one can recognise.

## Chapter 0120 - The Thing That Fails
The nineteenth to the twenty-first of September. The registrar says in a room that her own instrument has failed, in three parts: she cannot strike the certificate; **her instrument is a book that works by being read by somebody who has come to the yard, and about nine strangers came to the yard in nine weeks and one came back twice, and a woman two days' walk away is never going to come to the yard**; and **being the only person who can say no is not the same as being able to stop a thing.** He says **you have not failed** and enters it under the column for things that are not findings, and **nobody asked her why she keeps two lines apart, and this is the third time in nine weeks that nobody has.** The nineteen lines go into the public lot book and are not a finding and are none of the four and none of the six and none of the one. A stranger takes two sheets cold in the same minute, one saying a consent and one saying nobody could strike it, and **the strongest instrument this district owns and a drawer thirty-four miles away are the same instrument pointing in opposite directions.** The party of nine comes home with nineteen lines, two certificates, four descriptions of a job, a number of pans, and no salt. A fourth sheet goes up for four coppers saying that somebody else reads the number on the thirtieth of September. The month's last entry has no finding in it at all, and the protected category is closed at five and the count of not-askings stands at four with one standing refusal and neither moved.

## Chapter 0121 - The First Channel Fails
The twenty-first and twenty-second of September. A cart comes up the raised plank road with a sheet nailed to a stake, and the sheet carries a date, a place, a person and a reason, and the reason is nineteen words from a mason of about fifty who came for loam and did not find any: **the sill is a foot of loam laid by a man who is dead, and the flood year put nine feet of river over the whole flat in one season, and a foot of loam does not come back.** The salt stopped on or about the twelfth and was found on the twenty-first by a man who counts pans and would not say a number twice, and **the day it failed is entered as not known and is not a mystery.** A secretary of a cooperative of nine households had said on the seventh of September that the pan with nobody on it would be the first of the seven to fail and that nobody would know, and he says the second thing he wrote on the sheet: it cost a year of nobody's memory because there was nobody in it to cost. The party of nine goes east on the twenty-second, thirteen asked and nine chosen and ten went, four not asked, and for the first time the man who has spent ten weeks pricing everything in front of him is not on the cart. A mechanic of about forty-three offers to go and ask nine diggers and is refused in nine words.

## Chapter 0122 - Four Hundred and Two Thousand
The twenty-third to the twenty-fifth of September. A man of about forty-four with the second cut offers salt for **four hundred coppers**, unrecorded, the year his own, and says that if you want his name in a book he is not the man for your book. The cooperative of nine households offers **two thousand**, the only figure anybody on either bank has for a loom, and asks that the four of September be asked again in six things, and six things take a week. **Two thousand less four hundred is one thousand six hundred, and a man of thirty-four who mends fencing says the one thousand six hundred is the price of six things and six things are not a price anybody can charge because they are a week.** Nine households are asked in turn and all nine say yes, and **on the twenty-fifth, one day later, four of the nine hire the private channel anyway.** The finding: a price can be too low and still be a price, and a number a man did in eleven seconds off the price of a pound of salt stays that number when nine households put it behind it, and the only thing anybody in the room could state about what a loom should cost was **four answers and a week**.

## Chapter 0123 - Four Things She Would Not Sign
The twenty-sixth to the twenty-eighth of September. The party arrives on the flat; the bed is dry loam, the channel is a hole, the salt on the pan is grey and a thumb thick. A woman of forty-four who has done it eleven years hands over four sheets with no heading and no ruling and will not sign them, and the reason is thirty-one words: **a signature is a person agreeing to a thing, and she is not agreeing to anything, and one of the four is a man who asked her one question and she answered it wrong, and asking him to sign her sentence about it would be asking him twice.** The four things are not a document, not a claim and not a list of prices. The six things are put to the four of September and **the fourth, a man of about thirty-four who digs loam, was not asked, asked to be asked, and said no, and the season's sheet has three signatures and the form asks whether four people consented.** The council meets on the twenty-eighth with three rules, no officer, no clerk, no seal and no name on the door, and a man of about twenty-six stands up in the middle of it and says *I am the loom* and says he wants it said in the room and not in a cart.

## Chapter 0124 - The Twenty-Ninth and the Thirtieth
The twenty-ninth and thirtieth of September, and the first of October. **The boundary is asked from two rooms by two named people who do not hold it as a job and entered by a registrar who declines to enter it as a finding, and the man who holds the figure is thirty-four miles east and is in neither room.** The term runs to the thirty-first of March. **The figure of one hundred and eighty-three coppers is not read out by anybody, nobody is asked and nobody refuses**, and a man who does not read figures says in thirty-nine words that he read four numbers and not the fifth and would rather be nobody than be a man with no answer. A sheet goes up on eleven walls and three toll boards for four coppers, and a man of thirty-four who mends fencing puts up four of the eleven and refuses his own wall. **The first of October is reached and not resolved**: the season does not start, the bed of brine is full, a woman of thirty-eight came to work and was not in it, and the entry says it is not a finding and is not to be amended by anybody who wants a season. A stranger writes *what happens on the second* on the back of his own copy.

## Chapter 0125 - A Figure Against a Name
The second and third of October. Four people count for four hours and eleven versions of the answer come out of it, and a clerk of nineteen years strikes a figure of four years out of a woman's memory because a clerk was tired by the fourth hour, and enters that **the strike is the finding.** **The figure is twenty-one**: four, two, eleven and four. A year and a half is on a line of its own and is not added, because a year does not go twice. Two people are entered as not known and not asked. One frame took nothing off anybody. **The batch's one System panel arrives unasked in that room and says that a toll taken and not written down is still taken, that a figure of years is not a remedy and is not coppers and cannot be closed by a transfer because these years have no holder, that an address is not a bearer, and that a name put on a figure cannot be taken off it by the person it is put on.** He says yes twice, in six things, and the clerk enters that the two answers are the same two words and that the minute does not say they were the same.

## Chapter 0126 - One Sentence Across a Room
The fourth to the seventh of October. Two days and a bit on the road, and the figure of twenty-one goes onto a board of eleven in a yard and a stranger takes a copy cold. A reader at the counter who has read out a price, a rate and an unpaid figure for six years says she cannot finish the fourth kind of number, and a man of thirty-one who runs a cart of meal asks *is the twenty-one coppers* and she says **no.** The registrar asks him to a counting room, says nothing for nineteen minutes, and then says one sentence across the room, and he answers **Enter it as decided** in four seconds, and a clerk of nineteen years underlines nothing for the first time in ten weeks. **The romance moves one sentence and does not resolve, and the count of not-askings did not move because the sentence was said in a room and not in a kitchen.** The two lost days are priced: two days is eleven pounds of salt in a cellar and a fortnight in nine houses' plans, and there is no column in any book in this basin for a plan.

## Chapter 0127 - The Eighth Refusal
The eighth to the tenth of October. The reading is asked in the five things and the price is read out for him by a man of thirty-four who mends fencing, because a man who has not asked does not get a price read for him. **He says no in four seconds: the twenty-one years are not his and he is not going to open a second book to find out what one of them is worth, and the cost of the refusal is nothing at all.** The refusals are eight. Ivo Stenn reads the ledger out, reads it out again when asked what the intervals were, and his nineteen words go after the eighth refusal; the intervals have gone up on use for the third time in ten weeks and he will not call it anything else. **He then says yes to the name for the second time, because a figure with no name on it is a figure nobody can ask, and I would rather be a name on a figure than be a reason nobody asks.** The clerk refuses to put the refusal and the name in one column, because a refusal is a reading and a name is not a reading. A guild inspector of forty-four says the two certificates are in a drawer, both valid, neither strikeable, and that he knows his answer is not the answer.
